Not changed: Stripe prices and products.

## Phase 22
[2026-10-06] [23.1] Render speed (operator request 2026-10-06; production last 3 days: Shotstack p50 49 s / p90 113 s per render, ElevenLabs Music p50 6.9 s and ~5p per video, generated every time). (1) **Shotstack render callbacks**: each render request carries Shotstack's Edit `callback` URL (https://shotstack.io/docs/api/ and https://shotstack.io/docs/guide/architecting-an-application/webhooks/, read 2026-10-06: POST on done/failed with `{ type: "edit", action: "render", id, owner, status, url, error, completed }`, up to 10 retries with exponential back-off outside 200–399 or over 10 s, payloads NOT signed — "make a request to the API using your API key and the render ID"). Per-render token `?n=<16 random bytes>&s=<HMAC-SHA256>` (`providers/render-callback.ts`, constant-time check; secret `STUDIO_RENDER_CALLBACK_SECRET` ≥ 32 chars or HKDF from `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET`; on only when `APP_URL` is https; `STUDIO_RENDER_CALLBACKS=off`; platform account only, BYOC keys keep polling). Route `POST /api/studio/webhooks/shotstack` (`api/shotstack-callback.ts`): webhook rate limit per address, 401 bad token, 404 unknown render, 200 no-op for a render already recorded (replays), Serve "copy" callbacks ignored, the status is always fetched from Shotstack with our key and only a finished render (done or failed) sets a Redis wake flag (`providers/provider-wake.ts`, `studio:pwake:shotstack:<id>`, 15 min); 502 when Shotstack is unreachable so Shotstack retries. The worker waiting in `provider-run.ts` checks its flag every 1 s and polls at once when it is set; the provider_jobs row, cost settlement, retries and quality gate are unchanged (the worker still records the outcome). **Poll interval: before — every provider, Shotstack included, every 5 s (`DEFAULT_PIPELINE_TIMING.providerPollIntervalMs`, no backoff, 15 min timeout); after — Shotstack with callbacks every 20 s as the fallback for a lost callback (`CALLBACK_FALLBACK_POLL_MS`), every other provider and Shotstack without Redis / https still 5 s.** (2) **Multi-format renders**: compose-video already started each variant's render inside one Promise.all, but the first failure ended the job while the other renders kept running unawaited, and the retry submitted them again. Now every variant's edit is built first, then all renders are submitted together and awaited with allSettled; finished renders are recorded (render row + metadata.renders pointer, cost, mastering) and the first failure is rethrown, so the retry renders only the failed variant. Per-render provider jobs, retries, `STUDIO_PROVIDER_CONCURRENCY_SHOTSTACK` caps (none by default) and the quality gate are unchanged. (3) **Music reuse**: platform music library `music_library_tracks` (expand-only migration `20261011010000_music_library`; CI studio tables 88) keyed by music-prompt.ts's prompt key (closed vocabulary: mood / genre / energy / tempo; no customer text, language-neutral) and a duration bucket (request rounded up to 15 / 30 / 60 / 120 / 180 / 300 s; library tracks are generated at the bucket's full length). Until a key has `STUDIO_MUSIC_LIBRARY_SIZE` tracks (default 5) a video generates and adds its track (copied to `music-library/<key>/<id>.mp3` in the assets bucket: outside every organisation's prefix and outside the 30-day provider-output expiry); after that it reuses the least recently used one: a project AUDIO_MUSIC asset pointing at the library object, cost 0, no provider job, `metadata.music.status: 'reused'` (review screen shows it as a reused track). The project's own track still wins on a re-render; a missing library object is dropped and replaced; only ElevenLabs Music generations on the platform account join (never Storyblocks picks, never BYOC keys); `unsharedAssetObjects` keeps library objects when projects are deleted; `STUDIO_MUSIC_LIBRARY=off` restores per-video generation. Studio has no brand-kit music override or business music upload today (searched: none in schema or pipeline); upload videos keep their own soundtrack as before. Tests: render-callback, provider-wake, shotstack adapter (callback in the body, per-render token, 20 s fallback), callback route (11: bad / missing token, unknown render, replay no-op, payload never trusted, failed render, serve callback, bad body, 502, 429, off on http), provider-run-callback (callback wakes within 1 s; lost callback completes at 20 s; 5 s without Redis), music-library (buckets, settings, rotation, best-effort add), DB: parallel-renders (all 3 submits before the first poll; one failing variant → 3 + 1 Shotstack requests, 3 renders), music library (generate until N, then reuse in rotation at cost 0 with no provider job; project track first on re-render; missing object replaced; BYOC never added).
- Expected savings from the measurements: callbacks ≈ 2 s per render (average poll lag 2.5 s → ≤ 1 s) — wall of text 81 s → ~79 s, slideshow 112 s → ~110 s, hook + demo 135 s → ~133 s — plus about half the Shotstack status calls (a 49 s render: ~10 polls before, ~4 polls + 1 callback check after) (Edit API limit 300 a minute, https://shotstack.io/docs/guide/architecting-an-application/limitations/, read 2026-10-06). Music reuse ≈ 6.9 s and ~5p per video once a key has 5 tracks (every music-bearing format: ~74 s / ~105 s / ~128 s). Multi-format: no change to a single-format video; a failed variant no longer re-renders the variants that finished (one Shotstack cycle, p50 49 s, and its cost).
- NOT VERIFIED on production (no keys here). The 342 s of Shotstack time for one 3-ratio slideshow is the sum of three provider jobs that our code already submitted together; if the renders were run one after another, the cause is Shotstack's per-account concurrent-render allowance (renders over it are queued at Shotstack: https://community.shotstack.io/t/concurrent-renders/464, read 2026-10-06), which only a Shotstack plan change raises — operator: check the plan's concurrent renders.
- DECISION: library tracks are generated at the bucket length (an 8 s video asks for 15 s) so one track serves the whole bucket; the EDL trims and fades it as before.

[2026-10-06] [22.7] TikTok drafts (operator 2026-10-06: "build the TikTok draft"; Fastlane posts to the TikTok inbox by default so the creator adds a trending sound in the app). Docs read 2026-10-06: https://developers.tiktok.com/doc/content-posting-api-reference-upload-video (`POST /v2/post/publish/inbox/video/init/`, scope `video.upload`, body `source_info` only — FILE_UPLOAD with video_size / chunk_size / total_chunk_count — "Users must click inbox notifications to continue editing and complete posting") and https://developers.tiktok.com/doc/content-posting-api-reference-photo-post (`post_mode` `MEDIA_UPLOAD`: "Upload content to TikTok for users to complete the post using TikTok's editing flow. Users will receive an inbox notification", scope `video.upload`, title + description carried into the editing flow, no privacy_level). Expand-only migration `20261011010000_tiktok_post_mode`: nullable `platform_connections.tiktokPostMode` ('drafts' | 'direct'); NULL = direct, so every existing connection keeps posting directly; the OAuth callback CREATES new TikTok connections with 'drafts' (a reconnect, which updates the row, keeps the stored choice). `PATCH /api/studio/platform-connections/:id { tiktokPostMode }` (requireTenantContext → `studio:connections:manage` via withStudioRoute; TikTok, non-revoked connections only; audited `studio.connection.settings_update`; returns `uploadGranted`). Publisher (`platforms/tiktok.ts`): `TikTokPublisher.planFor` — drafts + `video.upload` → the inbox upload (video) / `MEDIA_UPLOAD` (photos), skipping creator_info; drafts WITHOUT `video.upload` → direct post with `draftsUnavailable: 'missing_scope'` + "Reconnect TikTok to send drafts…" in the result (never silent); otherwise the 15.A2 rule unchanged. The worker passes the connection's mode (`resolveCredentials`), stores `inboxReason: 'drafts'` and notifies the creator (new kind `tiktok_draft`, key `notifications.tiktokDraftSent`, with the keep-the-AI-label reminder because the inbox init cannot set `is_aigc`; also listed in notification preferences). UI: inbox uploads show the badge "Sent to TikTok drafts" instead of "Live" (Publications list, project publications, calendar) with a localised finish-in-the-app note; the TikTok card on Connections has the radio ("Send to TikTok drafts" / "Post directly") with "TikTok drafts let you add a trending sound before posting." and a reconnect alert when `video.upload` is missing; the Blitz keep sheet and the autopilot wizard (channels + summary) say "TikTok: goes to your TikTok drafts…". 17 keys × 11 locales (review lists regenerated). Demo: the TikTok connection sends drafts (PATCH handler), "Guess the hydration" shows "Sent to TikTok drafts". Tests: publisher request shapes (draft video body = `source_info` only, draft photo = `MEDIA_UPLOAD`), planFor matrix, missing-scope fallback (video + photo), worker (mode passed, metadata, notification text), API on PGlite (new = drafts, pre-22.7 reconnect stays NULL, PATCH 403/404/400/200, non-TikTok / revoked refused), Connections radio + hint + Blitz keep sheet components. No new table (CI `studio tables` count unchanged).
- Not verified live: production social keys are placeholders, so no draft was sent to a real TikTok account.
- Notification wording covers both inbox paths (drafts chosen and the 15.A2 fallback); analytics polling still starts after an inbox upload as before (TikTok returns no public post id until the creator posts).

[2026-10-06] [22.6] Fastlane format polish after the first production QA (hook + demo cmuwemk9u0001lr07ksoo489q, wall of text cmuwemk9l0000lr07fevog19f; both reached review, gate passed). (1) Baked-in black bars: the Veo text-to-video hook (720×1280) had a ~10 % black band across the top, shown behind the AI label and the hook text. Every VIDEO_CLIP is now measured once with ffmpeg `cropdetect` (https://ffmpeg.org/ffmpeg-filters.html#cropdetect, read 2026-10-06: `limit` default 24 of 255, `round`, `skip`; we sample 2 fps of the first 12 s, limit 24, round 2, skip 0, and read x1/x2/y1/y2 of the last line, which is the picture area across all sampled frames) through the existing media inspector and ffmpeg limiter (`pipeline/letterbox.ts`, `MediaInspector.cropBounds`). Bars under 2 % are ignored, a "bar" over 30 % (a dark scene) or an all-black sample means no crop, and a 0.5 % margin is trimmed past a detected edge; the result is stored as `metadata.letterbox` when a provider clip is stored (generate-asset.ts recordAsset: AI clips, hook clips, UGC actor clips, stock footage) and, for clips stored any other way (library footage, demo and other uploads, older assets), measured the first time they are composed and kept (`pipeline/letterbox-assets.ts`). The composer puts Shotstack's VideoAsset `crop` (https://shotstack.io/docs/api/#tocs_crop, read 2026-10-06: "Crop the sides of an asset by a relative amount", 0–1 per side) on that clip with the existing `fit: crop`, so the picture fills the frame; stacked hook + demo halves are cut from the picture inside the bars (`combineCrop`). Detection errors are logged and the clip is used uncropped — never a failed shot. An edit for a clip without bars is unchanged. (2) Hook caption 5.2 % → 4.2 % (the `hook_tiktok_classic` size; five lines over the creator's hair before) and hook lines at most 9 words (owner input, Create form, Claude prompt asking for 5–8; a longer answer is shortened at a clause end, else at 9 words without a dangling "and / the / for"; empty → retryable, so it is regenerated). (3) Wall of text: ~60 words in 8 s was unreadable. A block Claude writes (and a Blitz card's block) is at most 35 words on 6 short lines; the owner's own block keeps 60 words / 10 lines, and the video is on screen long enough to read its block at 3.5 words/s, never shorter than chosen, at most 12 s (`wallShownSec`, used for the project's formats, the script, the shot, the overlay and the quality gate's target); fonts stay as `wallTextFontPct`. Tests: cropdetect parsing, thresholds, crop combination, never-throw detection, compose-time measurement, EDL crop only with bars (existing EDL tests unchanged), Postgres pipeline (Veo band stored and cropped on every clip; an owner's 56-word block runs 12 s), hook shortening, 35-word Claude blocks. NOT VERIFIED: no live Shotstack render with `crop` + `fit: crop` together (each is documented separately) and no cropdetect run on the production ffmpeg 5.1 build here.

[2026-10-06] [22.5] Automations: weekly / monthly auto generation and posting at the lowest cost (operator 2026-10-06; plans/phase-22-blitz-automations.md). Table `automations` (expand-only migration `20261010010000_blitz_automations`; `content_plans.automationId`, `content_plan_items.format / angleId / fingerprint`). An automation is a recurring "Plan my month": each period is a `content_plans` row drafted from the business's angles and a snapshot of its content mix and run by the existing month-plan runner (throttled generation, allowance reservations, explicit post times held from the drip queue, auto-publish through the outbox and channel limit) — no parallel pipeline. Cadence 1–3 posts a day per network or 1–21 a week (spread over the week, `content-plans/weekly-slots.ts`), duration 1 week / 4 weeks / ongoing weekly / ongoing monthly, networks within the plan's channels, approval "Review in Blitz first" (default) or "Auto-approve and post", internal per-period cost ceiling (staff only). Slots get formats by weight, spread evenly (`blitz/allocate.ts`); when the allowance (UGC = 2), the cost cap or the ceiling cannot cover every slot the most expensive are dropped first (`cap_reached`); paid formats only when the owner raised their weight. Carousel slots skip TikTok until `STUDIO_TIKTOK_PHOTO_DOMAIN_VERIFIED=1` (shown "download only"), YouTube gets no slideshows or carousels, a mix with no format for the chosen networks refuses to start. Slots are made by `services/automation-items.ts` (carousels with the written posts, slideshows whose hook and closing slides are big text over a dimmed photo — new `content.headline`, Shotstack HtmlAsset shade `#80000000`, no CSS line-height — 15 s AI video, UGC with the 22.3 default creator). Runner `advance-automations` (every 5 min + kicks; one file; kill switch per automation): GENERATING → dedupe (90-day fingerprints, rewrite once, else `no_unique_content`) → REVIEW (notification) or straight to ACTIVE (generatePlan as the person who started it, membership re-checked) → COMPLETED; PAUSED by the owner (new generation held: `holdReason paused`), at rollover when the allowance is used up (notification + add-videos link), when billing access is not full, or when the person who set it up left; ongoing automations draft the next period 3 days before the current one ends with a fresh mix snapshot. Weekly insight: top post by views → card + notification, "Make more like this" nudges its format and angle (bounded). Routes `/api/studio/automations` (list, create), `/estimate` (no pence for customers), `/:id` (detail with periods and slots, PATCH draft), `/:id/{start|approve|pause|resume|cancel|more-like-this}`, `/:id/slots/:itemId` (keep / skip / reroll); audited (`studio.automation.create|activate|pause|resume|cancel|rollover|complete|slot|more_like_this`). UI: nav "Automations", list, wizard (channels → cadence → mix → approval → summary with posts per period, format split, lowest-cost note; staff see the typical cost via `useShowCosts`), detail (actions, pause reason, insight, slot calendar per period with format / status / reason / download-only), and `/blitz?automation=<id>` reviews a period one card at a time. Organisation purge, business hard delete and the data export cover the new tables; CI table count 87. Demo: two sample automations (active weekly with an insight, a period in review). Tests: allocation, weekly slots, slot platforms, Postgres runner (start, review, approve, auto, pause / resume, duplicates, rollover, allowance pause, paid formats), API routes. Nothing was rendered or posted for real.

[2026-10-05] [22.1] Hook + demo, Fastlane's main format (public developer docs https://developers.usefastlane.ai/#endpoints and founder demos, read 2026-10-05): a short silent reaction hook clip carrying ONE on-screen hook line, then immediately the business's OWN demo video, with music balanced against the demo's audio. New `VideoSourceType.HOOK_DEMO` and `UploadKind.DEMO_VIDEO` (same migration). Demo bank: `POST /uploads { kind: demo_video, businessId }` → PUT → `/complete` (existing upload path, storage and probe checks; 2 s – 5 min), `GET /api/studio/uploads/demo-videos?businessId=` (newest first); a demo is never claimed by one project — each project gets its own `video_assets` row on the object (`attachDemoUpload`), and retention keeps an object the bank still owns. `POST /projects { sourceType: HOOK_DEMO, hookDemo }` (zod `formats/hook-demo.ts`: demo upload id — default the newest —, optional hook line ≤ 12 words / one line / no emoji, hook source ai_creator | library, reaction surprised | curious | wait_what, layout sequential | stacked, audio mix balanced | demo | music, target 10–20 s, default 15); no demo → 422 `no_demo_video` (`NoDemoVideoError`, Fastlane's code); a brief is optional. Plan step `plan-hook-demo.ts`: the owner's line or one Claude line (frameworks call-out, result-first, contrarian, curiosity gap, mistake, question, story-open, pattern interrupt; brand voice, audience, business facts, demo file name), the text-safety gate, captions/hashtags, then per format: shot 1 the hook (3 s; `AI_CLIP` marked `providerRouting.hookClip`) with ONE TikTok-classic caption (white Montserrat 700, 3 px stroke, no box, anchorY 0.2 — below the AI label, above the face, which the prompt keeps in the lower middle; 0.5 on the seam when stacked), shot 2 the demo (`USER_UPLOAD`, trimmed to fill the target, its own audio). The hook clip goes through the EXISTING actor route (`actor_video`, router ACTOR_CANDIDATES Veo 3.1 Fast first; budgets, cost tracking, kill switch, account holds): a new `silent` actor request (empty line, prompt says nobody speaks; Veo body unchanged — same documented fields, docs/veo re-read 2026-10-05; Kling's dialogue-only actor path refuses it), 4 s, a fictional adult from the reaction preset only (never owner text, never a real person), the project's actor portrait as reference when `metadata.ugc.actorImage` is ready; ONE clip per aspect ratio (other scripts reuse it, `asset-reuse`, waiting without spending an attempt). No actor provider / provider unavailable → a FOOTAGE-licensed reaction clip from the library, else a clear failure (`hook_clip_unavailable`). Composition (`formats/hook-demo-edl.ts`, `pipeline/edl.ts`): hook muted, demo at the mix level, the music bed ducked under the demo's audio (new `musicUnderSpeechVolume`; a kept source audio now counts as speech for ducking), AI-generated label always on for a generated hook; stacked layout for 9:16 / 4:5 (hook top half + demo bottom half for the hook's length, then the demo full frame from where it was) with documented Shotstack VideoAsset `crop` / `trim` and clip `fit: contain` + `position` (`pipeline/edl-stacked.ts`; UNCONFIRMED until the first live render). One video of the allowance; no £ shown. ## Phase 21

[2026-10-06] [21.4c] Actor clip text guard, from production UGC QA video 3 (project cmuvx27w60000mo07w33r2xyu, Veo 3.1 Fast): the third actor clip came back with its own burned-in subtitles ("I walk in calm, / calm, c onfifin nt, / no scrambling!", misspelt, white, no stroke) under the creator, on top of our TikTok-classic captions. Cause: `actorClipPrompt` ended with "No subtitles, captions, on-screen text…", but `withDialogue` appended the quoted line AFTER it. (1) Prompt: `providers/dialogue.ts` `withDialogue` (shared by Veo and Kling, which built the same string itself) now ends every actor prompt with `NO_ON_SCREEN_TEXT` ("Do not show any subtitles, captions, written words or on-screen text anywhere in the video.") after the line. Prompt text only: the Veo 3.1 parameter table lists no negativePrompt (https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/veo, read 2026-10-06) and Kling's current API has no negative_prompt (kling.ts header); no request field was added. (2) Detection (`ugc/clip-text-guard.ts`): after an actor clip is stored, 3 frames (25/55/85 % of the clip, ≤ 512 px, the existing `media.frameJpeg`, so the ffmpeg limiter applies) go to Claude in ONE `text_generation` call through `runProvider` with the library ingest's image shape and a strict JSON schema: per frame "yes"/"no" to the operator's question (overlaid subtitles, captions, titles or watermarks; text physically in the scene ignored). Parsing is strict (one answer per frame, numbered 1…n, only yes/no); a malformed answer, a frame-grab failure, no text provider, an outage or a cost cap is logged and the clip kept as unchecked (`clipText.status: skipped`) — the check never fails a shot or a project. Model: the router's text_generation provider (the Anthropic adapter's configured model; the router has no per-request model choice, so no new model routing was invented); about 3 small images + 300 output tokens, cost recorded on the provider_jobs row and on `clipText.costPence`. (3) One retry: any "yes" → `generateActor` re-runs the identical actor request (same prompt, line, portrait + product references, seed) through `runProvider` (budgets, caps, kill switch, failover and cost tracking as for the first clip) and records the new clip with `regeneratedFrom` + `regenerateReason: burned_in_text` (asset metadata and the shot's routing snapshot); the retry is checked once more and never regenerated again. A failed regeneration keeps the first clip. (4) Review: when the kept clip of any UGC_ACTOR shot still has `clipText.status: text`, the quality gate adds `clip_text` with status `warning` (severity info; does not fail the gate, keeps the video from auto-approval via review-policy) and `detailKey: clipTextBurnedIn` {count, shots}; label `review.quality.codes.clip_text` and the detail in all 11 catalogues (merge-keys + review-list; catalogue check OK). (5) Switch: `STUDIO_CLIP_TEXT_GUARD` (unset/"true" = on, "false" = off; documented in .env.example, validated in setup/env-file.ts) or `PipelineConfig.clipTextGuard`. Tests: dialogue order for Veo + Kling (incl. no negativePrompt), parser/detector/guard units (strict parsing, in-scene wording, skips on every failure, one retry only, flag off → no frame or vision call), gate warning, and four DB-backed UGC pipeline cases (no text → no retry; text → exactly one identical regeneration; text twice → warning, no third clip; flag off → no vision call). Not verified on a live Veo/Claude call here (no keys locally).

[2026-10-05] [21.4b] UGC native look, from the operator's review of production UGC video 2 (project cmuujspax0000qx08qjelfi07: "the overlay still blinds the UGC creator and the poster in between does not work"; frames showed two-line `subtitle_box` captions in a #000000B3 box across the creator's chest, then a stock calendar photo with a boxed caption, a flat grey MOTION_GRAPHICS card and a flat grey TEXT_CARD, all silent). Observed in three of Fastlane's UGC showcase videos (https://www.usefastlane.ai/, inspected frame by frame for the brief on 2026-10-05): TikTok's classic captions (white, thin black outline, no box, about 3.5–4 % of the frame height, sentence case, placed where the face stays clear, one block at a time); the cut-away is real product footage (a hand holding a phone showing the app, handheld, full frame, no text card and usually no text); no flat colour or motion-graphic cards anywhere. Changed, for projects with `metadata.ugc` only: (A) new built-in presets `subtitle_tiktok_classic` ("TikTok Classic": Montserrat 800, 3.6 %, anchorY 0.70, #FFFFFF fill, #000000 3 px stroke, no shadow, backgroundType none, no fades, brandSubstitution false so a brand colour never replaces the outlined white) and `hook_tiktok_classic` (same look, 4.2 %, anchorY 0.11, under the AI label and above the face); voice captions use it on every burned-in platform in chunks of ≤ 6 words (`UGC_CAPTION_MAX_WORDS`); suggested labels in a UGC project use it (hook on the first actor shot ≤ 40 chars; B-roll one short line ≤ 40 chars at 0.70, template presets ignored); the composer draws no boxed headline in a UGC edit (`EdlInput.ugc`); `withReadableText` leaves box-less outlined styles alone (documented and tested); the AI label is unchanged. (B) `ugcTreatments` = UGC_ACTOR + IMAGE_STILL only; the script rules now say B-roll is the product being used, close-up, phone-camera style, 2–3 s, at most one between two actor shots, at most one short line, never a card; `applyUgcPlan` turns any TEXT_CARD / MOTION_GRAPHICS (or other) shot into a product still, budget-converted actor shots are 2–3 s stills with their line shortened (`oneShortLine`), B-roll is fitted to 2–3 s (wider only when short actor clips cannot otherwise fill the target: 2–5 s, then 1–10 s, so the ±2 s duration check still holds); a UGC still is the owner's product photo, else the business's image library, else a generated "phone-camera photo of hands using <product>" (`ugcStillPrompt`) through the existing IMAGE_STILL route (reuse, budgets, caps, kill switch, cost tracking unchanged), generic stock is skipped (only the last resort when the generator refuses), and a budget-converted UGC shot takes this route instead of `budgetStill`'s stock-or-card fallback; UGC stills always push in (`zoomIn`, `fit: crop`). Only actor shots speak, as before. The product photo is used as the still itself rather than as a reference image: Studio's `text_to_image` request has no reference-image field, and no provider API was added. Demo bread-box sample and the stubbed `ugc-actor` scenario script now use two 3 s product stills between three actor shots and no end card. Rendering on production is blocked (Shotstack out of credit), so this is verified through unit, EDL and DB pipeline tests only, not a rendered video.

- DECISION (yearly allowance): 8 per calendar month, not 96 at once — the existing engine counts calendar months, and "all at once" would let a yearly customer spend a year of provider cost in a week past every monthly cap.
- DECISION (weekly allowance): per ISO week (UTC), not per Stripe billing week — the same counting code with a 'YYYY-Www' window key; pack credit uses stay keyed by calendar month (cost-cap headroom).
- DECISION (what is an upgrade): more channels or a longer period (week → month → year) apply now; fewer channels or a shorter period at the period end (the old portal's `decreasing_item_amount` / `shortening_interval` rule). During a trial everything applies at once (nothing is charged until it ends).
- DECISION (channel limit): connecting is never blocked; the first-connected N platforms publish (oldest non-revoked connection per platform), enforced in `createPublication` (403 `channel_limit`, not retried by auto-publish; the reason shows on the Review screen).
- DECISION (long videos): hidden and refused for channel plans (allowance 0); the trial has 5 videos and no long video.
- DECISION (caps, internal): monthly = most videos one calendar month can hold × 241p (Seedance 2.0 full, 720p; coordinator 2026-10-04) × 1.25, daily = half; staff cost-cap override still wins; typical cost per STANDARD short 160p → 241p (per-video budget defaults kept on the 20.25 basis: owned by p21-tiered-models). Margins in runbook §5: profitable at typical use everywhere; negative at the cap for 1 channel monthly (−2 %) and every yearly plan (−7 % to −21 %); the 15-pack is −1 % at the £2.50 cap rate.
- Tests: `channel-plan`, `catalogue`, `entitlements`, `gateway-plan`, `channels`, `channel-migration`, `cost-adjustments`, `plan-quotas`, `pricing`, `stripe-setup`, `email-params`, `allowance`; Postgres: `billing-plan-change`, `billing-checkout`, `billing-webhook` (quantity / interval, schedule events), `test/api/billing-plan.test.ts` (routes, scoping, channel_limit on POST /publications), `test/api/admin-channels.test.ts`; components: pricing, Your plan, upgrade dialog, lock badge, admin panels, cost-hiding screens; e2e billing specs and the sweep updated.

[2026-10-04] [21.3] HD video on the full Seedance 2.0 (operator decisions 2026-10-04: first "tiered video models", then the same day "one per-channel subscription, £29 per channel a month, 8 short videos per channel in HD", which the billing rework maps to the internal STANDARD tier). `providers/seedance.ts`: every plan tier (BASIC kept on it for safety) uses the new `SEEDANCE_FULL_MODEL` (default `dreamina-seedance-2-0-260128`, the full Seedance 2.0) at 720p; `SEEDANCE_MODEL` (2.0 mini) now serves only requests without a plan tier and the fallback below; shots over 15 s still use 2.5. `pipeline/clip-budget.ts AI_CLIP_RESOLUTION` asks every tier for 720p (BASIC's 480p from 20.25 dropped). 1080p is kept as a code path: `VideoResolution` gains `1080p`, the adapter has 1080p frames and prices, and `STUDIO_SEEDANCE_RESOLUTION` (480p | 720p | 1080p, empty = the request's 720p; validated in the setup checker, both `.env.example` files) overrides every Seedance clip; no plan sets it. The request asks for the best resolution the chosen model offers (2.0 mini / fast stop at 720p). Docs read 2026-10-04 (pages last updated 2026-09-28): the Seedance 2.0 series tutorial (ModelArk/2291680: 2.0 offers 480p, 720p, 1080p and 4k, 24 fps, 4–15 s; mini and fast 480p / 720p only; activation needs a balance over $30, a $30+ AI Savings Plan or a resource pack), the create-task API (`resolution` "Default 720p; supports 480p, 720p, 1080p, and 4k" for 2.0; 720p 9:16 = 720×1280; 1080p frames 1920×1080, 1080×1920, 1440×1440, 1248×1664, 21:9 2206×946) and the pricing page (ModelArk/1099320: 2.0 input without video $7.0/M at 480p/720p, $7.7/M at 1080p; 2.5 $10.7 / $11.7; examples 2.0 720p $0.15/s, 1080p $0.37/s, mini 720p $0.08/s; the framesurfer September 2026 figures agree). Billed actual cost uses the task's reported resolution. Tests: tier → model and body shape, the resolution override and capping, 1080p frames and prices, costs, the Mini fallback, env (`SEEDANCE_FULL_MODEL`, `STUDIO_SEEDANCE_RESOLUTION`: options, registry, setup checker), runProvider with the real adapter (2.0 refused → Mini serves the clip, no account hold; account overdue → 2.0, Mini, then the next provider), cost model, budgets, golden CB-02 (BASIC now 720p), cost-regression baseline.
- DECISION: Mini fallback inside the adapter. A full-model submit refused as `auth` (ModelNotOpen, OperationDenied.ServiceNotOpen, …) or `insufficient_credits` (arrears, a used-up resource pack, out-of-credit wording; the existing 20.11 / 20.19 classification) is resubmitted at once to `SEEDANCE_MODEL` (720p at most) for that shot; only if Mini is refused too does the error reach the router (account hold, ops alert, then Kling, Veo, Runway, Luma as before). The full model is then skipped for 10 minutes (`FULL_MODEL_RETRY_AFTER_MS`, per process; refusals are free) and the event is logged (`Seedance full model refused; using the default model`). Rate limits, moderation, input and outage errors never fall back. A task that fails at poll time is not retried on Mini (the router's normal retry / failover applies). The cost reservation uses the model that will be called.
- DECISION: 2.5 kept for shots over 15 s: the full 2.0 has the same 4–15 s limit as Mini, and 2.5 (4–30 s) is the only Seedance model for 16–30 s; such shots are rare (AI clips are capped at 4 s by 20.25).
- COST (list prices, USD→GBP 0.75 / 0.79; 30 s short = 10 shots, clip budget unchanged: BASIC 3, STANDARD 4, PLUS 6 AI clips of 4 s). A 4 s 9:16 720p clip on 2.0: 86,400 tokens × $7.0/M = $0.605 → 46p (Mini 23p; at 1080p 194,400 × $7.7/M → 113p). Typical short: STANDARD (the channel plan) 4 × 46 + 49 = **233p / 241p** (was 141p on Mini); BASIC 181p / 187p; PLUS 325p / 337p. Long form STANDARD 3 min (24 clips) ≈ 1,357p / 1,411p. Mini fallback STANDARD 141p. Kling (26p) and Veo 3.1 Fast (30p) 720p clips now cost less than Seedance 2.0, so a failover is cheaper.
- DECISION: per-video budgets (`cost/project-budget.ts TIER_BUDGET_FLOOR_PENCE`, short form only): a typical short stays under half its budget at 0.79 and every failover under the 90% pause. STANDARD £4 → **£5** (241p), BASIC £3.50 → £5 (187p), PLUS / ENTERPRISE £6 → £7 (337p); long-form unchanged (£30; PLUS / ENTERPRISE £45; STANDARD 3 min 1,411p < 2,700p). Daily and monthly caps, the §P.2 typical costs, top-ups and the pricing page are untouched: the billing rework owns them.
- FOR THE BILLING REWORK: the per-channel plan's provider cost is about 8 × 233p = £18.64 a month per channel at 0.75 (£19.28 at 0.79) of the £29 price, before Stripe fees and infrastructure; the §P.2 STANDARD typical short (160p) and the current STANDARD caps (£15 a day, £73 a month) predate the full model, so size the new caps from 241p a short.
- OPERATOR: activate **Dreamina Seedance 2.0** in ModelArk → Model activation (each model is activated separately; the activation rules page lists 2.5, 2.0, 2.0 mini and 2.0 fast). A Mini resource pack does not pay for 2.0 clips (packs "offset charges only for the corresponding model"), so keep the balance or an AI Savings Plan funded. Until 2.0 is activated, clips silently use Mini at 720p (the log line above). Individual accounts allow 3 concurrent tasks per model (non-4K).
[2026-10-04] [21.1] Narration is never cut mid-sentence (operator request 2026-10-04, the "trimmer issue": production QA run 8 trimmed a 2.741 s line in a 2.5 s still after "to", losing "prep."). New pure `pipeline/shot-rebalance.ts` (`rebalanceShots`) and `pipeline/narration-rebalance.ts`, called at the start of `compose-video` (before captions and the EDL): a shot whose narration voice fit trimmed is lengthened to narration + 0.2 s (rounded up to 0.1 s) when its picture can play that long (stills, text and motion cards always; AI clip, avatar, stock and upload shots only when the stored clip's probed length — else the asset's recorded length — covers it), and the time is taken from other shots' slack, nearest first (the following shot before the preceding one), then from the script's ±2 s head-room (the same budget as voice fit's "extend"). One transaction writes the shot lengths, moves overlays that ran to a shot's end, and stores the narration's fit as `rebalance` (no `trimSec`, so the composer, captions, SRT and audio_sync all see the whole line). Trims that remain now cut at the end of the last sentence inside the shot when there is one (`trimDecision`, `endsSentence`: . ! ? … 。！？ ؟ । plus closing quotes), else at the last word; the fit records `sentenceBoundary` and `droppedWords`; `project.metadata.narrationShortened` lists them per script for the review screen; the quality gate's audio_sync reports them as a `warning` (`audioSyncShortened`, 11 locales).
- DECISION: rebalance runs at composition, not in the per-shot fit job: shots are generated in parallel, so only once every shot is terminal is the whole script measured; it is pure and deterministic (centisecond arithmetic, script order, fixed donor order), all-or-nothing per shot (a partial lengthening would still cut the sentence) and idempotent on a retried compose.
- DECISION: donor floor = max(MIN_DISPLAY_SEC 1.2 s, own narration + 0.2 s, overlays that end inside the shot, start + 1.2 s of overlays that run to its end, reading time at 3 words/s for unnarrated on-screen text). 1.2 s: short-form cuts run 1.5–3 s and below ~1 s a still or card reads as a flash; it also stays above the script's own 1 s minimum. Uploaded clips (USER_UPLOAD) never give time away; trimmed or unmeasured narration shots neither give nor (unmeasured) take.
- DECISION: audio_sync treats a remaining clean trim as `warning` (not `passed`, not `failed`): the cut is clean and the video usable, but words are lost, so it is kept from auto-approval (review-policy) and a person hears it first. A trim without word timing stays `failed`.
- SKIPPED (operator steps 2 and 3): letting a voice clip run into the next shot is not cleanly supported by the composer (edl.ts caps the voice clip at its shot and ducks music per shot; quality-sync, captions and the SRT all place narration inside its shot), and moving the boundary into a silent neighbour is exactly what the rebalance already does when the picture can play longer. There is no per-shot narration rewrite path to reuse (only the owner's own text edit, `updateShot`, and whole-script regeneration), so no automatic re-script was added.
- Tests: `shot-rebalance.test.ts` (production case 2.5 s still + 2.741 s narration → no trim, total unchanged and within ±2 s; donor order, floors, head-room, all-or-nothing, clip length, determinism), `narration-rebalance.test.ts` (writes, overlay retiming, probe and fallback, stale run, slideshow, malformed fit), `voice-fit.test.ts` (sentence-boundary trim, run 8 word fallback, `fittedShotSec`, `endsSentence`), `quality-sync.test.ts` (warning, mixed with a failure, rebalanced passes), `test/db/narration-rebalance.test.ts` on Postgres (PGlite locally).

[2026-10-03] [20.29] Load testing (operator requests 2026-10-03: many simultaneous video creations; 200 concurrent users). Simulated-provider harness on the real pipeline (`scripts/load/pipeline-load.ts`, `src/lib/studio/load-test/*`, refuses production), 200-user k6 scenario on the production compose stack (`load-test/k6/studio-users.js`, `scripts/load/seed-k6-accounts.ts`), `.github/workflows/load-test.yml`, `runbooks/load-testing.md`, results `ops/results/load-test-2026-10-03.md`. Verdict: 200 users hold on the 4 GB server with the new sizing (read p95 59–120 ms, 0 errors); with today's 2 GB container limits no errors but read p95 up to 666 ms and web at 90 % of its memory. Video throughput is bound by the Seedance individual account (~24 STANDARD shorts / hour), not the server.
- DECISION: per-provider in-flight caps in Redis (`providers/provider-concurrency.ts`), defaults Seedance 3 (BytePlus individual, operator figure) and Kling 20 (one video pack, Kling docs), no default for others; `STUDIO_PROVIDER_CONCURRENCY_<ID>="<max>[,org=<share>]"`, share default half rounded up so one organisation never takes the whole account; leases expire after providerTimeoutMs + 2 min (dead worker); fail-open on Redis errors like 15.C3. Waiters are counted and retries stretch from 10–20 s up to 6× with the queue.
- DECISION: a full provider or a provider `rate_limited` answer (429, Kling 1302/1303, Gemini RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED) delays the job without spending an attempt (`queue/rate-deferral.ts`; BullMQ `attemptsStarted − attemptsMade − 1` counts deferrals); 429s are bounded at 8 deferrals (~10 min), then ordinary retries. The breaker still counts 429s (a provider that keeps refusing is routed around).
- DECISION: `STUDIO_PROVIDER_OVERFLOW=queue` (default, cheapest) | `failover` (next provider while the first is full; measured ~25 % dearer, 2.7× faster on a 100-video burst).
- DECISION: customers see "Queued: … will start soon" (`metadata.providerWait`, review screen `QueuedNote`, 11 locales) while the run waits for a provider; no provider name is shown (20.11).
- DECISION: 4 GB sizing documented in `deploy/vps/.env.example` (web 1 GiB, worker 1.25 GiB, Postgres 768 MiB / 60 connections, Redis 128 MB, orchestration 4, assets 12, FFmpeg 2); defaults in compose unchanged (they still fit 2 GB). `PG_MAX_CONNECTIONS` empty already means 40 (`${…:-40}`).
- FINDING: the harness first reused clips across videos (15.B6 asset reuse, identical scripts); scripts are now varied so cost and time are realistic.
- PROPOSED (not run, needs the operator's cost approval): Phase 2 real-provider burst of 5 shorts on production, `scripts/load/real-burst.ts`, expected £7–10, hard cap £20.
[2026-10-03] [20.30] Full QA round 1 (operator: "check every pipeline… test all key components"; report `ops/results/full-qa-2026-10-03.md`). Whole Vitest suite run locally (5,710 passed, 771 DB-gated skipped for CI) and the existing Playwright specs left to the CI e2e job (Redis 7 + Postgres). Bugs found and fixed, each with a test: `/robots.txt` and `/sitemap.xml` were 404 (new `src/app/robots.ts`, `sitemap.ts`, `src/lib/seo/site.ts`: sitemap = home, pricing, six legal pages; robots disallows `/api/`, every signed-in prefix, `/p/`, `/meta/`); Create / Plan a month / first video / project Generate stayed clickable for a read-only organisation (the 402 gave no on-screen reason; a plan-less organisation keeps them, its click opens the choose-a-plan dialog): `account/create-access.tsx` disables them with `aria-describedby` and shows "Your account is read-only. Update payment to create videos." with a billing link, the server gate unchanged; the reference library returned the nearest videos with a weak percentage for a query with no real match (no relevance floor): `library/relevance.ts` `MIN_SIMILARITY` 0.25 (a title/tag word still matches) and the browse screen says "No close matches for “…”" with category buttons; approval workflow cards showed raw business ids ("business biz_1"): they show the business name. Demo mirrored to live: a one-word brief stops in DRAFT with `brief_too_vague` and three directions (same `isVagueBrief` rule), stock images are Pixabay only with the Pixabay licence note, legal readiness is ready with sign-up open, reference search uses the same floor and has two luxury-car samples. DECISION: `MIN_SIMILARITY` 0.25 is a conservative guess for text-embedding-3-small; tune it on the production corpus. Not done in this round (see the report): the stubbed end-to-end run of the 9-layer pipeline on Redis 7, an axe pass and the new per-screen Playwright specs.
[2026-10-03] [20.27] Admin plans, access, trial and cost caps per organisation (operator request 2026-10-03: a platform superadmin could not find how to change an organisation's plan, access or cost caps; the operator's own organisation was on a Stripe trial with the £15 total AI cap and could not generate). The server APIs existed (entitlements GET/PUT/DELETE, cost-caps GET/PUT, organisations list), but the override form lived under Admin → Billing behind an organisation id box, the Organisations page showed only policy and cost caps, and nothing showed the trial. Now Admin → Organisations lists every organisation with plan (resolved at read time: override expiry and grace clock, like the reader), access, trial state (until a date / paused by override / ended by staff), subscription status and AI cost this month, 50 a page (Previous / Next; `offset` and `pageSize` in the response); Open shows the existing organisation detail extended with a "Plan, access and trial" card beside the Cost caps card (same Section pattern; review policy below): effective tier, access, source; the trial with start and end, AI cost since it started against its £15 total and £10 a day with a meter; the stored row; the override; subscriptions), the override form (tier, access, custom limits, ENTERPRISE price with the live minimum, optional expiry, "End the trial now", required reason; access None / Read-only and ending a trial ask for confirmation), "Remove the override" (warns when it would bring a paused trial back), and the review policy and cost caps forms (new "Clear back to plan default": one audited PUT with both caps null). Mobile: search and id inputs are full width under 640 px, tables scroll, the detail stacks; dark mode uses tokens (amber trial text has a dark variant); RTL: logical classes only, arrows mirrored, `check-physical-css --fail` clean. 34 new strings in all 11 locales (merge-keys fragments; review lists regenerated). Tests: `entitlements.test.ts` (override pauses the trial, expiry brings it back, an ended trial never returns), new `cost-adjustments.test.ts` (running trial → £10 / £15 caps; override → none; ended → none with or without the override), new `admin-directory.test.ts` (`planSummary`), PGlite API tests in `test/api/billing.test.ts` (GET shows the running trial and its spend and the guard applies its caps; an override pauses and DELETE restores the trial caps; End trial now: audited with `trialEnded`, `capAdjustmentFor` returns no trial caps, also after the override is removed; a second end and ending with no trial are 400) and `test/api/p18-e-admin.test.ts` (resolved plan, trial state, paging, detail), component tests `organisations/org-plan.test.tsx` (list → open → PLUS + end trial → confirm → PUT body; access None confirm and cancel; tier-only save has no dialog; remove-override warning; cost caps clear; Arabic RTL), e2e `sweep.spec.ts` superadmin test ends a seeded organisation's trial from the Organisations tab. Runbook `billing-stripe.md` §3: how staff change a plan, access or caps and end a trial.
- FINDING: an active staff override already removed the trial caps (`resolveStoredEntitlements` keeps `trial` only when the source is `trial`; an override makes it `admin`), so the operator's organisation could have been unblocked with any override, but nothing in the UI said so and the caps came back when the override expired or was removed while Stripe still reported `trialing`. Separately, the cost guard (`cost/guard.ts`) applies a trial's caps before any cost-cap override, so raising the cost caps of a trialing organisation does nothing; the trial block and the runbook say so.
- DECISION: "End the trial now" is an explicit, permanent option on the override (`endTrial: true` on PUT …/entitlements; stored as `overrides.trial.endedAt` + `endedByUserId`, kept by the Stripe sync, which preserves an existing `overrides.trial`), not a side effect of every override, so a short goodwill override with an expiry does not silently end a trial. After it `resolveStoredEntitlements` never returns `trial` (so `capAdjustmentFor` has no trial caps and `plan-quotas` uses the tier's allowance), with or without the override; refused (400) when there is no trial, it was already ended, or Stripe no longer reports trialing. Audited inside `entitlement.override_set` (`trialEnded.endedAt`) rather than a new audit action, so the customer audit log keeps its existing label. Stripe is not changed: the Stripe trial still ends and charges on its own date (the gateway's `endTrialNow` would charge the card at once, which nobody asked for).
- DECISION: "End the trial now" on its own (no tier, access, limits, price or expiry) ends the trial without writing a new, empty, never-expiring override (code review); the form remounts after every save so its fields start empty; the organisations list orders by createdAt then id so pages never repeat or skip.
- DEMO (coordinator 2026-10-03): the demo's admin sample data has the operator's case, "PostMind (operator)" on a Stripe trial at £14.96 of its £15 cap; the demo handlers (`demo/api/handlers/admin-plan-state.ts`, `p18-admin.ts`, `p18-admin-billing.ts`) return the trial, the resolved plan and `endTrial`, and the tour's What's new and Screens pages link to it. Checked in the built demo: list → open → Plus + End the trial now → confirm → "Ended by staff"; 375 px (no horizontal scroll), dark theme and RTL.
- RISK (code review): `putAdminEntitlements` and the Stripe sync (`recomputeEntitlements`) both read-modify-write `org_entitlements.overrides` without a lock; a webhook processed in the same instant as a staff save could drop the other's change (e.g. `endedAt`). The window is milliseconds and the nightly reconcile does not touch `trial`; re-saving fixes it. Follow-up if wanted: an optimistic `updatedAt` check on both writers.
- e2e: the sweep spec's superadmin test was extended but not run green locally (the local dev-server run stopped at sign-up, before any 20.27 step, a local environment issue); CI runs it.
- DECISION: the Billing tab keeps its by-id Entitlements panel (core mode and direct ids) and shares the same components; the Organisations list now shows the organisation id under the name instead of the slug (staff copy ids into support tickets and the cost report).

All six `content/legal/en-GB/*.md` pages now say "Last updated: 3 October 2026"; no `[[…]]` markers added (`scripts/legal/check-ready.ts`: all six `ok`, so production sign-up is not closed). Acceptable use: new 2.4 (AI presenters; real people's images only with consent; Seedance refuses real faces; no getting round provider safety filters) and 2.5 (AI people disclosure), 7.4 names video budgets, restricted-topic checks and provider safety filters, 8.1 says no scanning service and finished videos are not scanned. Cookies: the `theme` local-storage key (next-themes default key from `src/app/providers.tsx`) added; other cookies re-checked against `auth/config.ts` and `i18n/locales.ts`; Google Fonts in the editor unchanged (`overlays/overlay-frame.tsx`). DPA: 5.1 points to the sub-processor list with data, location and safeguard; 8.1 project-file deletion timings; 8.2 deletion on termination (read-only 90 days, scheduled deletion stops processing and wipes tokens, daily hard-delete job after 30 days, backups within a further 30 days); new 8.3 provider copies; 10.1/10.2 Singapore, Malaysia and Australia with the DPF / Addendum / IDTA wording; 10.3 copies; Annex 1 "restricted-topic checks"; Annex 2 R2 default location, KMS eu-west-2, RBAC, 30-minute support sessions, content safety without a scanning service, per-video budgets, pgBackRest (WAL archiving + full/differential, AES-256 before upload, separate R2 bucket, ≤ 30 days), provider copies. FILL-IN.md: trial-limit and per-video-budget rows and a note on the `LEGAL REVIEW` comments. Checks: `npx prettier --check content` clean, `vitest run src/lib/legal src/components/marketing test/unit/i18n-catalogues.test.ts` green, `check-ready.ts` all ok; no page mentions Hive or a content-safety provider.
- LEGAL REVIEW items (HTML comments `<!-- LEGAL REVIEW: … -->`, not shown on the site, not fill-in markers): (1) subprocessors: the safeguard column states the mechanism relied on, not signed contracts; confirm DPF certification (with the UK Extension) and UK Addendum / IDTA availability per provider; per-row comments on Cloudflare, Resend, Stripe, Sentry, Anthropic, OpenAI, BytePlus (Singapore/Malaysia), Kling (Singapore; address, processing location, DPA), Google Gemini API, Runway, Luma, HeyGen, ElevenLabs, AssemblyAI, Shotstack (Australia), Browserless; (2) Hetzner location: the deploy runbooks recommend Falkenstein/Nuremberg (Germany) while the production server is recorded as Helsinki, so confirm (both EEA); Cloudflare DNS removed because the production domain's DNS is recorded as hosted elsewhere; (3) privacy 7.2 and DPA 10.2: transfers to Singapore, Malaysia and Australia are newly stated; transfer risk assessments needed; (4) acceptable-use 2.4: the operator's brief says HeyGen avatars use the customer's own photo, but the code uses one operator-chosen presenter (`HEYGEN_AVATAR_ID`) and has no customer photo upload, so the clause is written conditionally; confirm when that feature ships and that the stock presenter's licence covers customers' adverts; (5) the earlier 20.23/20.24 BytePlus and Kling comments are kept.

Adding `remark-gfm` to `remarkPlugins` fixes it (also in the demo, which uses the same component).
