[2026-10-01] [20.13] Captions and hashtags (operator request 2026-10-01: "a trending caption and relevant hashtags — minimum of 5 hashtags, 1 of which must be the business hashtag"; owner hashtags on every generated, approved or auto-published post). Business hashtag + "always include" hashtags per business: new table `business_hashtag_settings` (add-only migration `20261006010000_captions_hashtags`, which also adds `content_plan_items.postCopy`; CI studio table count 80 → 81; purge-tables + business hard delete), default derived from the business name (`hashtags/business-hashtag.ts`: "AheadAi" → #AheadAI, legal suffixes dropped, short acronyms kept, shouting tamed, non-Latin scripts kept as written, ≤ 30 characters, at least one letter), `GET/PUT /api/studio/businesses/:id/hashtags` (400 with `details.problem`; audit `studio.business.hashtags_update`), Business → Hashtags tab (`?tab=hashtags`), a read-only note on Create, Plan my month and Publish. Policy (`hashtags/policy.ts`): business hashtag first (so it is among the three YouTube may show above the title), then the always-hashtags, then the chosen/AI ones, de-duplicated case-insensitively, at least 5, never over the platform maximum (chosen ones trimmed first, the business hashtag never), topped up when short from the project's other suggestions, the brief keywords and the business profile (niche, industry, regions, products, services, audience; `hashtags/pool.ts`). Generation: the ideation call now also returns `socialPosts` (caption + hashtags + title per target platform; same Claude call through the router, cost-tracked) with the business facts, the hashtags Studio adds itself, restricted topics (brand kit + profile) and the UK calendar days of the next three weeks (the month planner's list); slideshows get one text_generation call at plan time (`generateSlideshowCopy`, cached on the slides' text; a failure never stops the slideshow); month plans draft a caption + hashtags per post in the same planning call (`postCopy` per platform on the item, copied to the project when generated). All are fitted per platform and stored as the project's caption suggestions (`metadata.captionSuggestions`, key now includes the hashtag policy), so "Suggest captions" also works for slideshows. Editing: `GET/PUT /api/studio/projects/:id/post-copy` (owner edit in `metadata.postCopy`; PUT needs publication:write and also rewrites the project's SCHEDULED, not-yet-sent publications; audit `studio.project.post_copy_update`) and `PUT /api/studio/content-plans/:id/items/:itemId/post-copy` (audit `studio.content_plan.item_copy_update`). The Publish panel and the plan item editor use a chip editor (add/remove/reorder; business + always hashtags locked but movable; counter "n hashtags (at least 5, at most N)"; caption length against the platform limit in characters or YouTube bytes; #Shorts shown as platform-added), labelled "Suggested" / "Edited by you" with a note that suggestions are not live trend data. Publish time (`services/post-copy.ts` → `createPublication`): business + always hashtags added and the list topped up; a person's POST /publications with fewer than 5 (or more than the platform allows) after that is a 400 (`details.code` hashtags_minimum / hashtags_maximum); Studio-initiated posts (auto-publish, scheduled, drip, month plan) never fail on the count, use owner edit → target caption → generated suggestion → brief hook, record `metadata.hashtagsToppedUp`, and still fit the caption (15.A9). Publishers: hashtags were already in TikTok `title`, Instagram/Facebook `caption`/`description`, X `text` and LinkedIn `commentary` ({hashtag|\#|tag}); YouTube now also sends them as `snippet.tags` within the documented 500 characters (https://developers.google.com/youtube/v3/docs/videos#snippet.tags, read 2026-10-01). i18n: `hashtags.*` + `business.screen.tabs.hashtags`, 48 keys × 11 locales (Arabic six plural forms), review lists regenerated; catalogue and physical-CSS checks pass. Demo: Business → Hashtags, per-platform post copy on review screens, plan items with captions and hashtags, sample publications with #LeedsSourdough first and ≥ 5 tags, What's new entry. Tests: business-hashtag, policy (merge, de-dup, limits per platform, X fitting with five 30-character tags), pool, copy-prompt (no "trending", UK moments), caption-suggestions, YouTube tags + Instagram caption payloads, hashtag editor / settings panel / publish panel components, `test/api/p20-captions-hashtags.test.ts` (routes, 400s, top-up, scheduled rewrite), golden `test/golden/captions-hashtags.test.ts` (generate → approve → auto-publish carries #AheadAI + #LeedsEats + the generated tags; without generated copy it tops up instead of failing); existing publication tests now send five hashtags (`test/helpers/hashtags.ts`).
- DECISION (trending): no platform Studio publishes to offers trending hashtags within the current approvals (official docs, read 2026-10-01). Instagram `ig_hashtag_search` only returns the id of a hashtag you already know, and `top_media`/`recent_media` return posts for it (needs the Instagram Public Content Access feature through App Review; 30 unique hashtags per 7 days): https://developers.facebook.com/docs/instagram-platform/instagram-graph-api/reference/ig-hashtag-search, https://developers.facebook.com/docs/instagram-platform/instagram-api-with-facebook-login/hashtag-search. TikTok has no trending endpoint in the Content Posting API, and the Research API is for non-commercial academic researchers only: https://developers.tiktok.com/products/research-api/. The YouTube Data API has no hashtag endpoint (`videos.list chart=mostPopular` gives trending videos, not tags): https://developers.google.com/youtube/v3/docs/videos/list. LinkedIn and Facebook have none (Meta deprecated its Trending API in 2018: https://developers.facebook.com/blog/post/2018/07/02/a-platform-update/). X has `GET /2/trends/by/woeid/:id`, but under pay-per-use billing ($0.010 a request, https://docs.x.com/x-api/getting-started/pricing), not verified for the operator's Basic plan. So nothing in the UI, prompts or captions says "trending": the copy is "timely, platform-native" from the business profile, the post and UK calendar days, labelled "Suggested". X trends could be added behind a provider interface if the operator approves the X billing change.
- DECISION: X `maxHashtags` 2 → 5 (X documents no hashtag cap; its help centre only recommends two as best practice; the operator's rule is five), so X posts carry exactly five and the caption is shortened to fit 280 (suggested tags ≤ 30 characters; the top-up prefers the shortest; five 30-character tags still leave about 125 characters). TikTok, LinkedIn and Facebook stay at a maximum of 5 (so exactly 5), Instagram 30, YouTube 15 (+ #Shorts). ops/compliance-matrix.md regenerated.
- DECISION: the hashtag settings are stored per (organisation, business) in their own table so they work in core mode too, where Studio has no business names (no derived default there until the owner sets one); standalone mode derives the default from `studio.businesses.name`.
- DECISION: non-Latin business names keep their own script (#مخبزالشام), no transliteration; a name with no letters has no default and Business → Hashtags asks for one.
- DECISION: no automatic "#ad": posts go out on the business's own accounts, which the CAP Code treats as obviously marketing; the prompt forbids posing as an independent customer or reviewer and inventing prices, offers, quotes or claims, and restricted topics are passed in. The platform AI labels stay as before.
- DECISION: a Studio-initiated post with no generated copy, no profile and few keywords may still go out with fewer than 5 hashtags (logged in `metadata.hashtagsToppedUp`); Studio never invents tags it has no source for.
- NOT VERIFIED: no live Claude call with the larger ideation schema (scripted adapter only); not checked against live platforms (linking of non-Latin hashtags, YouTube `snippet.tags`). This worktree's `node_modules/stripe` was missing and was copied from a sibling worktree (same 22.6.2) to run typecheck and the API suites.
[2026-10-01] [20.14] Posting schedule: daily/weekly, chosen/system/interval times, max 4 a day (operator request 2026-10-01: the calendar's "Drip queue" panel offered three one-click plans and a raw slot list; it should be "every day, choose how many posts a day and when, or let the system pick times / post at intervals, max 4 a day; the same for N posts a week"). Pure resolver `src/lib/studio/posting-schedule.ts` (zod + zoned-time only; shared by the API, the calendar editor, onboarding and the demo): a schedule `{ mode daily | weekly | custom, timezone, postsPerDay 1–4, postsPerWeek 1–28, days, timesMode choose | system | interval, times, intervalBy user | system, intervalStart, intervalMinutes 30–720, windowStart, windowEnd }` resolves to the drip queue's existing weekly slots (weekday + HH:MM + IANA zone), so the outbox, open slots, `heldSlots` and the month planner keep reading `drip_queues.slots` unchanged. Daily: postsPerDay on every chosen day (all 7 by default; a day can be unticked to skip it). Weekly: N posts over the chosen days (default spread 1 → Wed, 2 → Tue/Thu, 3 → Mon/Wed/Fri, 4 → Mon/Tue/Thu/Fri, 5 → weekdays, …), floor(N/k) a day with the remainder spread evenly, validated days ≤ N ≤ 4 × days. Times: "I'll choose" (one input per post of the busiest day, ascending, ≥ 30 min apart; a lighter day takes the first times), "Pick times for me" (the month planner's 12:30 / 09:00 + 17:30 / + 12:30 / + 20:00 — `DAILY_POST_TIMES` moved here and re-exported from content-plans/slots.ts so "N a day" and a daily schedule give identical times, tested), "At regular intervals" (every N from a start time, never past midnight; or spread evenly across posting hours, default 08:00–21:00). Problems are coded (days_empty, week_too_many, week_too_few, times_count/order/gap, interval_overflow, window_order, window_too_short) with English API messages and translated UI copy. `upcomingSlots` moved into the module (drip-queue.ts delegates), plus `previewTimes` (next 7 days), `inferSchedule` (reopens pre-20.14 slots: one zone and the same times on each posting day → daily/weekly "I'll choose", else custom), `fitTimes`. Data: add-only migration `20261006090000_drip_queue_schedule` (`drip_queues.schedule JSONB NULL`; no new table, CI table count unchanged). API: PUT `…/drip-queue` takes `schedule` (and/or `slots`); a daily/weekly schedule is validated and resolved on the server (client slots replaced), a body with only slots is stored as a custom schedule, every slot list ≤ 28 and ≤ 4 on any weekday (zod superRefine); GET returns `schedule` (null for old rows); audit metadata gains mode and timesMode. UI: `calendar/schedule-editor.tsx` (How often: Every day / Times a week; posts a day 1–4 or a week 1–28; day chips Mon–Sun; times: Pick times for me (read-only + "Change these times") / I'll choose / At regular intervals (Every…Starting at, or Posting hours from…Until); time zone select from `Intl.supportedValuesOf`), `schedule-preview.tsx` (summary "4 posts a day · every 3 hours from 09:00 · Europe/London" and the next 7 days in the schedule's zone), `schedule-model.ts` (pure state transitions), `drip-queue.tsx` (problems as an alert, Save schedule, "Advanced: edit each time (n)" — the old per-slot list, collapsed; editing it makes the schedule custom). Native radios/checkboxes with labels (keyboard arrows), logical CSS only, wraps at 320 px. Onboarding `posting-plan-card.tsx`: Every day (posts a day) or Times a week (posts a week, default days) with the system times and "Save posting plan". The 20.3 preset buttons are gone (`drip-presets.ts` kept for the demo seed and DRIP_HORIZON_WEEKS); old keys removed, `calendar.drip.schedule.*` and `onboarding.plan.save` added in 11 locales (real translations, Arabic six plural forms), review lists regenerated, catalogue and physical-CSS checks pass. Demo: PUT validates and resolves with the API's `dripQueueInput`; the seeded bakery queue carries its weekly schedule. e2e sweep: the calendar step now sets "Times a week · 3 · Pick times for me" and saves. Tests: resolver (`posting-schedule.test.ts`: daily system/choose/interval/window, skip days, weekly spread/validation/choose, defaults, fitTimes, inferSchedule, zod, DST across 25 Oct, next-7-days, month-planner consistency for "N a day" and "use my posting times"), components (`calendar/drip-queue.test.tsx`: each mode, validation messages, time zone, reopen saved schedule, Advanced custom and > 4 a day; calendar and onboarding tests updated), API (`test/api/p20-posting-schedule.test.ts`: daily/weekly resolve on the server, schedule stored and returned, 400s, capability, legacy slots-only body, NULL schedule rows, month-plan defaults).
- DECISION: posts of one day are ≥ 30 minutes apart (MIN_POST_GAP_MINUTES = DEFAULT_STAGGER_MINUTES), so with the default platform stagger one post's second platform goes out before the next post.
- DECISION: the server is the source of truth for daily/weekly slots (it resolves the schedule and ignores client slots), so stored slots always match the stored schedule; a slots-only body (older clients, the API) becomes `{ mode: 'custom' }`.
- DECISION: in weekly "I'll choose", the inputs cover the busiest day and a day with fewer posts uses the first times; interval/system schedules are validated on the busiest day (fewer posts only widen the gaps).
- DECISION: skipping days in daily mode was cheap (the weekly day chips) and is included.
[2026-10-01] [20.12] Auto-publish: accounts vs platforms; no blocking without connected accounts (production bug, operator screenshot 2026-10-01 on /create, Slideshow tab, business AheadAi, no connected accounts: "9 platforms · Short · auto-publish · Options" and "Choose at least one account to auto-publish to, or turn auto-publish off." although "the accounts have been selected already"; plus the operator's request the same day: "There are no auto-publish options in slideshow like the videos"). Root cause: (1) the auto-publish toggle and account pickers lived behind Options and were not rendered at all on the Slideshow tab (`create-screen.tsx` `!isSlideshow`), but `validateCreate` (`create/body.ts`) still enforced `autoPublishAccountRequired` for a slideshow whenever `autoPublish` was true — once it had been switched on (on the Video tab, before switching tab) a slideshow could not be created and nothing on screen could turn it off; `buildCreateBody` ignored auto-publish for slideshows anyway, so slideshows could never auto-publish or be scheduled from Create although the API (and month plans) supported it; (2) nothing distinguished "platforms" (the 9 format chips the operator had selected) from "accounts" (connected social accounts, of which the organisation had none: the platform app keys are dummies), so the summary still said "auto-publish" and the message asked for "accounts" the user took to be the chips; (3) with no connection the toggle could still be switched on (every picker disabled), and the API accepted `AUTO_ON_APPROVAL` / `SCHEDULED` with no target (an approved video then had nowhere to go). Fix, Create (Video, Slideshow and Upload alike): a "Post to" section in the main form (`create/auto-publish-option.tsx`), not behind Options. No connected account for the business → "No connected accounts — videos/slideshows are saved for review, not posted." with "Connect an account" (/connections), auto-publish forced off, the schedule and "next free slot" disabled with "A schedule posts automatically, so it needs a connected account." + link, and the summary says "no connected accounts: saved for review". Accounts connected → "Auto-publish when approved" defaults on when an account can post one of the chosen platforms; one picker per chosen platform that has an account, pre-selected when the business has exactly one there (`automation.ts` `resolveAccounts`: a choice for another business or a disconnected account is dropped, so switching business never posts to the wrong account; "Don't auto-publish" wins; picking settles the toggle, so un-picking everything asks instead of silently turning off); "Not posted automatically (no connected account): X, …" for the others; the summary says "posts to N accounts" or "saved for review". Validation names what is missing: `autoPublishAccountRequired` "Pick the TikTok or LinkedIn account to post to under “Post to”, or turn auto-publish off." (the chosen platforms that have an account), new `autoPublishNoMatchingAccount` "None of your connected accounts posts to the platforms you chose. Add TikTok, connect another account, or turn auto-publish off.", new `scheduleNeedsAccount`, reworded `scheduleNeedsAutoPublish`. Slideshows and uploads send `publishPolicy` / `autoPublish` / `scheduledStartAt` exactly like videos (the schedule is no longer disabled for slideshows). Month planner (`/plans/new`): an account is asked for only for platforms that have one; platforms without one are listed ("Made but not posted automatically …"), and with no account at all "No connected accounts: the posts are made and saved for your review instead of being scheduled." + Connect link; the draft editor and the "Generate and schedule" confirmation say the same for a plan with no account. Review → Publish: with no account for any variant, one notice "No connected accounts yet, so there is nowhere to publish. The video stays saved here." + Connect link instead of a bare "Publish now (0)". Server: `POST /projects` (non-template) with `AUTO_ON_APPROVAL` or `SCHEDULED` and no target → `400 auto_publish_account_required` (`AutoPublishAccountRequiredError`, `automation/targets.ts` `assertTargetsForPolicy`, the client's rule); a target connection that is not this organisation's or platform's → `400 auto_publish_account_unavailable` (`AutoPublishAccountUnavailableError`; format and duplicate problems stay `validation_error`); both codes are in `errors.codes.*`, so every locale gets a sentence instead of a generic 400. `POST /content-plans`: `targets` optional (default `[]`), partial coverage allowed (a target for a platform outside the plan is still refused). Copy: "platforms" = formats to render, "accounts" = connected social accounts to post to, in 11 locales (real translations; 20 keys added, 5 reworded: `create.screen.summaryAutoPublish`, `create.problems.autoPublishAccountRequired` and `scheduleNeedsAutoPublish`, `create.autoPublish.legend` "Post to", `plans.new.problems.accountRequired`); review lists regenerated; catalogue and physical-CSS checks pass. Demo: the plan handler accepts plans with partial or no accounts. Runbook: review-publish-automation.md (the two codes and "Post to"; plan step 9). Tests: `create/create-accounts.test.tsx` (the exact screenshot state: Slideshow, all 9 platforms, brief, no account → "Create slideshow" creates the project without auto-publish and the summary says "no connected accounts"; Video, Slideshow and Upload each with (a) no account, (b) accounts that post none of the chosen platforms, (c) matching accounts pre-selected and sent; switching business), `create-automation.test.tsx` and `create-screen.test.tsx` updated (default on with the only account; slideshow targets), `body.test.ts` (problem codes, slideshow and upload bodies, the screenshot state), `automation.test.ts` (`hasConnectedAccount`, `publishablePlatforms`, `resolveAccounts`), `plans.test.tsx` and `plan-model.test.ts` (no-account and partial plans, the editor notice and confirmation), `publish-panel.test.tsx` (no-account notice), `api-i18n.test.tsx` (both codes translated), `test/api/p20-autopublish-accounts.test.ts` (both codes for videos and slideshows; a slideshow with targets → stored → approved through the real route → one outbox row; a scheduled slideshow; a plan with no account generated with every post REQUIRE_APPROVAL / MANUAL / unscheduled / not pre-approved; a partial plan scheduled to the covered platform only; a foreign account refused), `p20-content-plans.test.ts` updated; e2e sweep: /new now expects the no-account notice on every source (its owner has no connections).
- DECISION: a month plan with no connected account is not blocked. It is drafted and generated like any plan, but every post is made with `reviewPolicy REQUIRE_APPROVAL`, `publishPolicy MANUAL`, no `scheduledStartAt`, no targets and `metadata.contentPlan.preApproved false`, so each post waits in review (item status READY, "Needs your review") and nothing is posted. Why: the operator's "auto-post with a review window" decision cannot auto-post without an account, and silently scheduling posts that can never go out (or failing Generate) is worse than making them and saying so; the owner is told on the form, in the editor and in the "Generate and schedule" confirmation before anything is made. A plan with some accounts schedules the covered platforms and renders (but does not post) the others. The plan's times still count as held drip-queue slots.
- DECISION: on Create, auto-publish defaults on only when an account of the business can post one of the chosen platforms and a target results (a platform's only account is pre-selected; with several the owner picks, nothing is guessed), and is always off with no connected account. Approval still gates every post (the organisation's review policy), so default-on posts nothing unreviewed.
- DECISION: the API enforces "auto-publish or a schedule needs an account" only for requests that set `publishPolicy` themselves; a project template's own publish defaults are left as they were.
- REVIEW: live publishing to real accounts is unverified (the platform app keys are dummies); the sweep's new /new assertion was not run locally (Playwright against a production build).
[2026-09-30] [20.11] Provider failover on account limits; no raw provider errors for customers (production bug 2026-09-30: a website scan on studio.postmindai.pro showed the customer `Anthropic reported a problem: the request was rejected. 400 {"type":"error",…"You have reached your specified API usage limits…"}`; the router listed OpenAI as the text fallback but a 400 was classified `invalid_request`, not retried and not failed over). Classification from the providers' docs (read 2026-09-30, cited in code): new class `account_limit` beside `auth` and `insufficient_credits` (`ACCOUNT_ERROR_CLASSES`, `providers/account-errors.ts`). Anthropic (platform.claude.com/docs/en/api/errors and /api/rate-limits): 400 invalid_request_error whose message begins "You have reached your specified (workspace) API usage limits" → account_limit; 429 rate_limit_error with `details.error_code` enforced_spend_limit_reached (tier spend cap) → account_limit; both carry the stated "regain access on … UTC" time as `retryAt`; 402 billing_error → insufficient_credits; 401/403 → auth; the adapter now reports the API's `error.message` (never the raw JSON body) plus status and request id. OpenAI (developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/error-codes): 429 code credit_balance_exhausted or type insufficient_quota → insufficient_credits; organization_spend_limit_exceeded, project_spend_limit_exceeded, organization_usage_limit_exceeded → account_limit (text, images, embeddings and transcription share the classifier). Every HTTP adapter keeps 401/403 → auth and 402 → insufficient_credits (Luma 402, ElevenLabs insufficient_credits / quota_exceeded, HeyGen credit codes and Hive V3 405 were already mapped). The ElevenLabs health check (`/v1/user/subscription`) answering "missing the permission user_read" now reports healthy for TTS and music, which do not need that scope. Breaker (`circuit-breaker.ts`, `circuit-breaker-redis.ts`): `tripAccount` opens a provider at once (no five-failure count) until the stated resume time or for 15 min (capped at 32 days), then one half-open trial; shared through Redis (`openMs`, `holdClass`, `holdReason`, `holdUntil`, `holdSince`; HMSET so Redis 3 works) with the in-memory fallback; `accountHolds()` for the Admin Centre. `tracked.ts` trips it for account classes at submit and poll. Failover (`pipeline/provider-run.ts`): `runProvider` re-routes in the same call when the chosen provider fails with an account problem (`excludeProviderIds`, skip reason `account_unavailable`), each provider with its own provider_jobs row and cost; when nothing is left, `ProvidersUnavailableError` (a NoProviderAvailableError, code `service_unavailable`, 503, not retried), so callers that degrade without a provider (captions, content safety, library ingest) keep doing so. Every text call site already routed through `runProvider` (ideation, script, script safety, scan classification, month plan, caption suggestions, slideshow text, library analysis and blueprints, overlay preview, consent check, voice profiles, regenerate script); none calls Anthropic directly, so all of them fail over. The OpenAI text adapter already sends `outputSchema` as a Responses API json_schema (strict when compatible) and returns `metadata.json` like Claude; the callers' zod validation is unchanged. Website scan: when no embedding provider is available the scan still succeeds with the profile and images and notes "service_unavailable: images were not indexed for search yet". Customer text: stored reasons for account problems and "no provider" are `service_unavailable: …` without provider text (`describeError`; budget-only routing failures unchanged); `FailureReason` shows "Our AI service is temporarily unavailable, so this could not be finished. Please try again later — our team has been alerted." (11 locales), reads older `<provider>/<auth|insufficient_credits|account_limit>: …` rows the same way, and no longer shows a provider's own text after a provider_failure sentence (social platforms' reasons still shown); API errors (`toErrorResponse`) never carry a ProviderError's message (fixed sentence + providerId / errorClass) and answer `service_unavailable` with a friendly sentence; stock-image errors in a scan's list show `<provider>/<class>` instead of the provider's text (`publicErrorText`). Alerts (`providers/account-alerts.ts`): one staff notification (new kind `provider_alert`, keys `notifications.providerAccountProblem` / `providersUnavailable`, 11 locales) per provider, class and UTC day, deduplicated in the database by dedupeKey and in process, plus one naming every provider when a capability is exhausted; posted to `OPS_ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL` (Slack) only by the call that created it; logged at error level; never fails the job. Admin Centre → Providers shows "Held: <problem> until <time> UTC" and the provider's message (`admin-health.ts` `accountHold`; healthy is false while held); the demo shows a held Runway. Runbook: provider-outage.md "Provider account problems". Tests: Anthropic / OpenAI / ElevenLabs mappings with the documented bodies, account-errors, breaker holds (memory, Redis fail-safe, shared Redis incl. Redis 3.0), tracked holds, runProvider failover (the real Anthropic adapter's usage-limit 400 → the real OpenAI adapter returns the same JSON; a held provider is skipped later without a call or a second alert; both exhausted → ProvidersUnavailableError, not retryable, friendly reason, three alerts; Runway auth → Luma; rate limits do not fail over), alert dedupe and webhook, failure-reason parsing and rendering (en-GB, ar, zh-Hans), the scan panel with the production string (no JSON), API error bodies, admin health service / API / panel; integration on Postgres (`test/api/website-scan.test.ts`): a scan whose Anthropic returns the usage-limit 400 completes through OpenAI with provider_jobs rows for both, and with both accounts out fails as service_unavailable with no provider text in the API. Results: typecheck, lint, format:check, catalogue and physical-CSS checks pass; full vitest on PGlite: 546 files, 5,524 passed, 1 failed (an admin-health API expectation missing the new `accountHold` field; fixed and re-run green with the other changed files, 25 files / 464 tests); demo build and `next build` pass.
- DECISION: account problems hold a provider at once rather than counting towards the 5-failure breaker: retrying a key with no credits only delays the customer and repeats the error. The hold ends at the provider's stated resume time when it gives one (Anthropic spend limits), else after 15 minutes, followed by the normal single half-open trial.
- DECISION: when every provider for a job is out because of account problems the job is not retried (the operator has to act and is alerted); the customer tries again later. The friendly message does not say "nothing was charged": a video's monthly allowance is counted when generation starts, so that would not always be true.
- DECISION: customers never see a provider's own text (it can be a raw JSON body); it stays in provider_jobs, logs and the Admin Centre. Social platforms' refusal reasons are still shown because they tell the customer what to change.
- DECISION: alert channels reused — staff in-app notifications (+ the notification webhook) and `OPS_ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL`, deduplicated per provider, class and UTC day; the existing Prometheus `StudioProviderCircuitOpen` also fires while a provider is held. No new Prometheus rule.
- DECISION: not matched — Anthropic's "credit balance is too low" message, which is not in Anthropic's error documentation (CLAUDE.md rule 2); a 402 billing_error is. Runway's docs define no credit error (401 → auth only). AssemblyAI, Shotstack, Storyblocks and Hive keep status-based mapping (401/403 auth, 402 credits).
- KNOWN LIMITATION: async content safety (`content-safety-async.ts`) routes and submits outside `runProvider`; an account error there holds the provider and the job's next attempt routes around it, but it does not fail over within the same attempt.
- NOTE: in production OpenAI also has no credits today, so text jobs will show the friendly unavailable message (and an alert naming both providers) until the operator tops up OpenAI or Anthropic's limit resets at 2026-10-01 00:00 UTC.

[2026-09-30] [20.10] QA sweep: every page and workflow of a production build (`next build` + `next start`, standalone mode, PGlite, e2e-job env with stand-in legal texts, email off, placeholder Stripe/S3) driven by a new Playwright sweep, `e2e/sweep.spec.ts` (skipped without DATABASE_URL like the happy path; runs in the CI e2e job): signed out (landing, pricing, six legal pages, sign-in/up, forgot/reset password with no token, verify email, signed-out redirect, 404); a user with no organisation (all 14 workspace pages and /account/export go to /welcome, profile and security stay, then the wizard: organisation, business, brand kit saved, connect skipped, first video to the plan gate); an owner (every nav page and settings tab, project filters and a project's five panels, publications filters, analytics ranges and metrics, calendar month navigation and a "3 a week" posting plan, business tabs, approval workflow create/delete, organisation and profile saves, billing Monthly/Annual, a seeded library reference: detail, "Make one like this", "Same video, my content", feedback, member invite, website scan, export request, notifications, dark theme, Arabic RTL and German); a superadmin with 2FA and no organisation (2FA set-up through the UI with a computed TOTP, every Admin Centre tab). It fails on error banners, page errors, console errors, unexpected 4xx/5xx API answers and off-site redirects (`E2E_SWEEP_SOFT=1` only lists them, `E2E_SWEEP_SHOTS` saves screenshots). Bugs fixed, each with a regression test: (1) a superadmin with no organisation saw "PostMind staff only" in the Admin Centre because every admin API answered 403 no_organisation (PR #46 only unblocked the page): admin routes (`studio:admin:*`) now get a staff-only context for staff/superadmin with 2FA and no membership (organisation `postmind-platform`, no memberships, staff capabilities only, no plan gate, separate cache key; workspace routes still answer no_organisation), `identity/standalone.ts`, `tenant.ts` `ResolveOptions`, `api/route.ts`; (2) /account/export showed "Couldn't load this / You don't have permission" without an organisation: it now goes to /welcome like the other organisation pages (`no-organisation-redirect.tsx`); (3) the 18 Admin Centre tabs overflowed a centred row at 1280 px and Kill switch, Features and Re-drive were hidden under the sidebar and unclickable: the row wraps (`admin-centre.tsx`); (4) Connect TikTok/YouTube/X/LinkedIn without the platform's app settings failed with a 500 "Something went wrong": GET /platform-connections reports `configured` per platform, the screen says "<Platform> is not available yet: Studio's <Platform> app still needs its settings. Ask your administrator." (as for Meta) and oauth-init answers 501 with that sentence (`oauth.ts` `oauthConfiguredFromEnv`, `connections.ts`, `platform-card.tsx`; new key `connections.notConfigured`, 11 locales); (5) dark-theme visitors hit React error #418 (hydration mismatch) on every page load from the header's theme toggle: it renders the light state until mounted (`app-shell.tsx`, SSR + hydrate test); (6) no not-found or error boundary existed: unknown URLs showed Next's English-only 404 and a render error a blank "Application error" page; new translated `src/app/not-found.tsx`, `src/app/error.tsx`, `src/app/(studio)/error.tsx` (inside the shell, Try again, error reference; `errorPage.*`, 11 locales); (7) English users saw "Request failed (500)" for any unexpected server error (body `{ error: 'internal_error' }` with no message): the catalogue sentence is used (`client/api.ts`); (8) the Queues and Dead letters tabs showed an opaque 500 when Redis rejected at once (refused, too old): now a 502 naming the Redis error, like the timeout (`admin-health.ts` `asRedisUnavailable`, `dead-letter.ts`). Docker image: `test/unit/dockerfile-runtime.test.ts` now also scans src/ for folders read by path at runtime (`join(process.cwd(), 'content', …)`, `join(root, …)`, path literals such as `'infra/s3-lifecycle.json'`) and fails unless the runtime stage copies them or they are listed as host-only with a reason (deploy/, infra/, ops/, load-test/); content/ and messages/ are copied. CI e2e job: placeholder AWS keys (not secrets) so the sweep's library reference can presign its preview offline. Results: typecheck, lint, format:check, i18n catalogue and physical-CSS checks pass; full vitest: 532 files, 5,416 tests passed and 7 failed in a run with the QA app's env (Stripe and email variables set change what billing and email tests expect; `test/queue/bullmq.test.ts` needs Redis ≥ 5); the 6 affected files pass with only DATABASE_URL, as in CI (bullmq skipped locally); Playwright: happy path and the sweep pass except the export request, which needs Redis ≥ 5 (local Redis is 3.0; CI has 7); `next build` and the demo build + demo tests pass.
- FINDING (not a bug): the sign-in redirect Location looked like `http://localhost:<port>/sign-in` only because the local server was started with `-H 127.0.0.1`; with `next start -p <port>` (Dockerfile, CI) Next relativises it to `/sign-in?next=…`. Middleware unchanged.
- KNOWN LIMITATIONS (not fixed): the worker cannot run locally (BullMQ needs Redis ≥ 5), so generation, website scans, exports and publishing past the queue are covered by the existing integration tests and CI only; billing with placeholder Stripe keys shows "Price unavailable" and disabled plan buttons (checkout, top-ups and the portal are covered by the fake-Stripe integration tests); a plan-gated action (scan, generate) shows the upgrade dialog and also an error toast (duplicate feedback; 61 toast call sites, left as is); a request that cannot enqueue because Redis is down answers 500 "Something went wrong on our side"; the calendar's reschedule dialog needs a scheduled publication, which needs a render, so it was not clicked (component tests cover it); library previews need real storage (placeholder keys presign URLs that do not resolve).

[2026-09-30] [20.9] Plan my month: batch-generate videos and slideshows and auto-schedule (≤ 4 a day) (operator request 2026-09-30; decisions the same day: auto-post with a review window and ONE summary email, AI-planned varied topics the owner edits first, a video/slideshow slider defaulting to 50/50). Data: `studio.content_plans` (organisation, business, creator, who scheduled it, start date + days (≤ 31) + time zone + [windowStart, windowEnd), postsPerDay 1–4 or useDripSlots, videoShare 0–100, platforms + AutoPublishTarget[] targets, language, brand kit, plan tier, requestedCount, cappedReason allowance | cost_cap, holdReason kill_switch | cost_cap | daily_limit, draftError, status DRAFTING → DRAFT → GENERATING → SCHEDULED → COMPLETED / CANCELLED, summaryEmailedAt) and `studio.content_plan_items` (slotAt, kind VIDEO | SLIDESHOW, angle, title, brief, slide text, calendarDay, status PLANNED | QUEUED | GENERATING | READY | SCHEDULED | POSTED | HELD | FAILED | SKIPPED | REMOVED, statusReason, projectId, quotaReservation); migration `20261005010000_content_plans` (expand-only), both tables in the organisation purge and the business hard delete. Slots (`src/lib/studio/content-plans/slots.ts`): an explicit post time per item — daily times 12:30 / 09:00 + 17:30 / + 12:30 / + 20:00 in the plan's zone, or the business's drip-queue slots inside the window — at most 4 a local day; times other videos hold and times under 2 h away are skipped; the default start is tomorrow, or the day after the business's latest active plan. Mix (`mix.ts`): the video share spread evenly; a rotation of how-to, product feature, behind the scenes, offer and "why customers choose you", never the same angle twice in a row; one seasonal post per UK calendar day in the window (`calendar-days.ts`: fixed days, Easter-based days, bank holidays, Black Friday, Small Business Saturday), on the day or up to 3 days before. Allowance (`allowance.ts`): the count is capped at the remaining monthly short allowance plus unused top-up credits (warn mode: no cap) and at what fits under the monthly cost cap (org override, env, catalogue, trial caps, credit headroom per credit used) at the typical cost per post; nothing left → 403 quota_exceeded with a top-up message. Draft (`services/content-plan-draft.ts`, job `draft-content-plan`): Claude through the provider router (text_generation, costed to the organisation), 20 slots per call, each call told the topics already planned; system prompt `prompts/month-plan.md` (read at runtime), user prompt from the website scan's business profile, the brand kit (tone, audience, restricted topics), style memory, the last 90 days' posts to avoid and the language; the answer is schema-validated and trimmed, one entry per slot or a retryable ProviderError; after the last failed attempt the plan is an editable DRAFT with draftError and "Write the missing posts" (`/redraft`). Editing (`services/content-plans.ts`): topic / brief / kind / slide text, add a post at a free time inside the window (≤ 4 a day), delete, reorder (topics move, times stay), "New topic" for one item (≤ 60 per plan); 10 drafts per organisation per 24 h and one DRAFTING plan per business. Generate (`services/content-plan-run.ts`, `POST …/generate`, needs project:approve + publication:write): one project per item through createProject (its validation, targets, tier gates and default budgets) — VIDEO as a 15-second BRIEF project, SLIDESHOW as a custom slideshow of text-card slides (hook, 3–5 points, CTA) — with reviewPolicy AUTO_APPROVE, publishPolicy SCHEDULED, scheduledStartAt = the item's time, the plan's auto-publish targets and `metadata.contentPlan { planId, itemId, preApproved: true }`; each item's allowance is reserved through checkGenerateQuota (locked; top-up credits first-in first-out; without entitlements the same `metadata.quotaSlot` is written so the items count each other); the first item it cannot cover stops the run cleanly (that and every later item SKIPPED 'allowance'). Resumable: the project id is stored before the quota check, and a GENERATING plan may be generated again. Runner (job `advance-content-plans`, every minute on studio-orchestration, plus a kick after generate): keeps item statuses in step with their projects (pipeline → GENERATING, needs a person → READY, content or script safety or a pending safety review → HELD, approved with a scheduled publication or pending outbox row → SCHEDULED, published → POSTED), starts queued items earliest first as low-priority batch jobs (`generateProject` gained `{ batch }`), at most STUDIO_CONTENT_PLAN_CONCURRENCY (default 2) per plan and STUDIO_CONTENT_PLAN_DAILY_STARTS per UTC day platform-wide (default 30 on Hive V3, otherwise unlimited), holds the plan (never fails it) during a workspace/global kill switch or a cost-cap pause, skips a queued item whose time is under 45 min away (allowance given back, draft project archived), marks the plan SCHEDULED once every item is settled and sends ONE `monthPlanned` email (new template: category account, typed params url / postCount / needsAttention / startDate / endDate, dates as noon UTC of the plan's local days, idempotency key per plan, 11 locales) to whoever pressed generate (standalone users; core mode logs and skips), then COMPLETED after the last post time. Review window: DELETE on a scheduled item (publication:write) cancels generation and its scheduled publication, drops pending outbox rows, sets publishPolicy MANUAL and preApproved false and frees the time; PATCH on a generating or scheduled plan swaps the topic (old project detached, new one prepared at the same time, allowance checked again); cancel plan does the same for every item not yet out (publishing ones are kept and counted). Plan times are held drip-queue slots (`heldSlots` includes active plan items), so the queue never offers them; `GET …/drip-queue/upcoming` also returns `planned` (items still being made). UI (11 locales; catalogue and physical-CSS checks pass; review lists regenerated; RTL tested): `/plans/new` (start date, length, 1–4 a day or "Use my posting times", the video/slideshow range slider with aria-valuetext, platform chips, one account per platform, allowance and spending panel with "Add a top-up", typical cost per post, estimate line), `/plans/:id` (drafting progress; the draft editor by day with Edit / Move earlier / Move later / New topic / Delete / Add a post, capped notice, cost estimate and allowance use, "Generate and schedule" behind one confirmation, "Discard plan"; then counts by status, held-by-safety and hold notices, each post with its status, reason, Open, Swap topic and Remove, Cancel plan) and `/plans`; entries on Create ("Or plan a whole month…"), the calendar header ("Plan my month") and the end of onboarding; the calendar draws plan posts still being made as dashed "Planned" links. API: `GET|POST /api/studio/content-plans`, `GET …/defaults`, `GET …/:id`, `POST …/:id/redraft|generate|cancel|reorder|items`, `PATCH|DELETE …/:id/items/:itemId`, `POST …/:id/items/:itemId/regenerate` (requireTenantContext → requireCapability → execute, zod, audit `studio.content_plan.*`). Demo: `demo/api/handlers/p20-plan-month.ts` (the real slot and mix maths; a half-made October plan with scheduled posts on the calendar, posts still being made and one held by safety; a new plan drafts and then generates over a few reads), routes /plans, /plans/new, /plans/:id, What's new (two 20.9 items), "Everything built", the monthPlanned email preview; demo rebuilt. Docs: runbooks/review-publish-automation.md step 9 (how it works, costs, Hive V3, stopping a plan) and verification queries, email-resend.md, .env.example. Tests: unit (calendar days, slots incl. DST, mix, prompt and the prompt file's rules, allowance and cost capping incl. credit headroom, item status mapping, review-policy pre-approval, the client plan model), components (form, drafting, editor, plan view, list, Arabic RTL, calendar planned markers), integration (`test/api/p20-content-plans.test.ts`: every route, capabilities, validation, the allowance cap at draft and at generate, remove / swap / cancel, redraft, status sync, runner holds and skips), golden (`test/golden/month-plan.test.ts`: 3 days × 2 posts, 3 videos + 3 slideshows through the real pipeline on scripted providers → every post auto-approved and scheduled at its own time → one email → a post removed), demo (`test/unit/demo-plan-month.test.ts`).
- DECISION: explicit post times per item (project.scheduledStartAt) instead of letting the drip queue hand out slots at approval: approval order is random when a month generates in parallel (Halloween could post after Bonfire Night) and a queue cannot exceed its weekly slots; explicit times also work with no posting times at all and never change the owner's saved queue. The plan's times count as held drip slots.
- DECISION: "Generate and schedule" is the owner's approval of the plan's items (metadata.contentPlan.preApproved). It waives only the "first 10 videos" trust threshold; content safety, script safety, quality checks, ENTERPRISE (always review) and the organisation's auto-approve policy still hold a post for a person, and a held post is never published.
- DECISION: projects are created and the allowance reserved when the owner presses generate (with their tenant context), not lazily in the worker; the worker only starts generation. Removing or swapping an item that has not started gives its reservation back.
- DECISION: planned videos are 15 s (the short end of the prompt's 15–30 s, the least AI clip time); slideshows are text-card slideshows (no images needed, always renderable; images can be added in the slideshow editor before the post time).
- DECISION: cost estimates use the catalogue's typical cost per short video for videos and the slideshow per-project budget (£1.50, an overstatement) for slideshows; "at most" is every item's budget cap.
- DECISION: the summary email is category "account" (not unsubscribable: the owner asked for the plan); no in-app notification was added.
- DECISION: the prompt lives in `prompts/month-plan.md` as asked (read at runtime; prompts/ is already copied into the runtime image); the Layer 1–2 prompts stay in code.
- FINDING: allowance is counted in the month a generation starts (plan-quotas.ts); a plan submitted in the last hours of a month whose items start after midnight UTC counts those items in the new month too. Rare with the default pacing; not changed here.
- FINDING: on Hive V3 (about 100 requests a day for the whole platform) a 120-post plan on 3 platforms needs about 360 scans; the default daily start limit of 30 spreads it over days. Production volume needs V2 Enterprise.
- NOT VERIFIED: no live Claude call with the month prompt (scripted provider in tests); prompt quality and chunk latency need a staging run with a real business profile.

DECISION: the slides are product screenshots in both themes (only the displayed theme's image loads); the flow note says they come from the demo with a fictional bakery's sample data.

Studio now ships every font a render can name in `public/fonts/` (16 TTFs, 25,065,080 bytes, about 24 MB; Noto Sans SC alone is 17.8 MB): Anton, Bebas Neue, Caveat, DM Sans, Fraunces, Inter, Montserrat, Noto Sans, Noto Sans Arabic, Noto Sans Devanagari, Noto Sans SC, Permanent Marker, Playfair Display, Poppins, Roboto Mono, Space Mono, all from github.com/google/fonts at commit 9710da1 (SIL OFL 1.1; Permanent Marker Apache 2.0), licences in `public/fonts/licenses/`, sources, sizes and checksums in `public/fonts/SOURCES.md`. `next start` serves them at `/fonts/<Family>.ttf` (next.config.ts headers: Cache-Control public, 1 day + stale-while-revalidate 7 days; CORS *); the Docker runtime stage now copies `public/` (it did not before). `src/lib/studio/fonts-host.ts` `fontsBaseUrlFromEnv`: STUDIO_FONTS_BASE_URL when set, else `APP_URL/fonts` (web and worker both have APP_URL: compose.yml x-app-env, render-deploy table), used by create-deps for overlays, the pre-render, brand cards and thumbnails (`THUMBNAIL_FONT_FAMILY`). STUDIO_FONTS_BASE_URL moved to the OPTIONAL section of deploy/vps/.env.example (setup:check still wants https when it is set; it was never in requiredEnvForModes); runbooks deploy.md, vps-deploy.md table, go-live.md §11 (docs-site/go-live.html regenerated), render-deploy.md table and .env.example updated. Middleware fix: the page-guard matcher had `\.` inside a plain string, i.e. an unescaped dot, so its lookahead matched every path of two or more characters and the optimistic sign-in redirect ran only for `/` (server-side checks were unaffected); now `\.`: font files, API routes, `_next` and `/p/` stay unmatched and signed-out app pages redirect again. Tests: `test/unit/self-hosted-fonts.test.ts` (every family from the presets, default style, script → Noto map, onboarding picker, thumbnail font and every fontFamily/fontPrimary/fontSecondary literal in src/ and demo/ has its file; each file is TrueType, named exactly as the family Studio asks for, weight class 400 and default wght 400; licence and SOURCES.md row per file; total size; Dockerfile copies public/), `src/lib/studio/fonts-host.test.ts`, `src/middleware.test.ts` (Next's own matcher: /fonts/*.ttf never matched, a signed-out font request passes, /projects redirects), env-file test (optional; empty = ready).
- DECISION: variable Google Fonts files are shipped unchanged when their default instance is Regular and their family name is exact (weights stay selectable for Shotstack; FFmpeg drawtext draws the default instance, Regular). Montserrat and Noto Sans SC default to Thin and Fraunces to Black, and Montserrat / Noto Sans SC / DM Sans carry an instance in the family name, so those four were rebuilt with fontTools (`scripts/fonts/regular-default.py`: weight axis 400–900 with default 400, names fixed; OFL Modified Versions, no Reserved Font Name involved). google/fonts no longer has static weights for variable families; Poppins, Space Mono, Anton, Bebas Neue and Permanent Marker ship static Regular only (bold is synthesised or unavailable).
- DECISION: no italic files (an italic overlay gets a slanted or upright Regular). Space Mono ships because the demo overlay fixtures name it.
- FINDING: brand cards and slideshow text fall back to CSS 'Arial', sans-serif when a kit has no font; Arial cannot be redistributed, is not hosted and was not before (unchanged). A brand kit that names a family not in public/fonts (free text) still fails the render; runbooks/deploy.md says how to add one.
- OPERATOR: nothing to set; leave STUDIO_FONTS_BASE_URL empty. Unverified until a staging render: that Shotstack fetches https://<domain>/fonts/*.ttf and honours weights in the variable files; check a Montserrat 900 hook, an Arabic and a Mandarin overlay at GATE.
[2026-09-30] [20.6] Hive V3 (self-serve) content safety, V2 path kept (operator decision 2026-09-30: "Both"). Contract from Hive's docs (read 2026-09-30): https://docs.thehive.ai/docs/visual-moderation-playground (the playground's "API Docs"): `POST https://api.thehive.ai/api/v3/hive/visual-moderation`, `authorization: Bearer <Secret Key>`, JSON `{"input":[{"media_url"}]}` (or `media_base64`) or multipart `media`; one object per request; images jpg/png/webp/gif and video mp4/webm/m4v; 200 MB (URL/multipart), 20 MB base64, video ≤ 60 s; synchronous response `{task_id, model, version, output:[{extra, classes:[{class_name, value}]}]}`, one entry per frame for video with `extra` `frame_index`/`timestamp`; playground/V3 default 100 requests/day. /docs/visual-content-moderation: V3 "for developer testing ONLY", same class names and scores as V2; /docs/image-generation-models and /docs/hive-vision-language-model-vlm: the V3 key's **Secret Key** is the Bearer token, the Access Key ID is only an identifier (so the operator's two values = Access Key ID + Secret Key; the V2 403 was expected); /reference/error-codes + /common-errors: 429 rate limited, 403 key, 405 insufficient balance. No V3 async/callback, status or cancel endpoint is documented. New `providers/hive-config.ts` (`HIVE_API_VERSION` v2|v3; empty = v3 when only `HIVE_V3_SECRET_KEY` is set, else v2; `HIVE_V3_MAX_FRAMES` 1–60, default 10), `providers/hive-v3.ts` (client, error wording, sampling), `providers/hive-scan.ts` (shared per-class maxima; V2 `class/score` and V3 `class_name/value` map to the same scan, so the block/review policy in quality-checks.ts is unchanged). `HiveAdapter` takes `apiVersion` (default v2: unchanged behaviour); V3 is always synchronous: renders ≤ 60 s = one request with the presigned render URL (Hive samples 1 fps); longer renders = N evenly spaced frames grabbed with the existing ffmpeg `frameJpeg` (media-probe.ts) and uploaded as multipart images, one request each, aggregated by max; any failed frame fails the scan. The quality gate uses the V2 async path only when the platform is on v2 (`usesAsyncHiveScan`, `PipelineConfig.hiveApiVersion`). 429 → retryable `rate_limited` with "about 100 requests a day … fails closed", logged as `content-safety scan rate limited`; the job retries and the video is never published unchecked; 403 names the Secret Key vs Access Key ID; 405 → `insufficient_credits`. Registry: `providerKeysFromEnv` picks the key per version (`ProviderKey.apiVersion`; BYOC org keys stay V2). setup:check: the Hive keys in deploy/vps/.env.example's REQUIRED section are one-of (`HIVE_ONE_OF_KEYS`, `hiveChecks`: the selected key must be set; short V3 value → "Access Key ID?" reminder; unused other key → reminder), `HIVE_API_VERSION`/`HIVE_V3_MAX_FRAMES` validated (OPTIONAL). Staging gate: Hive test runs with either key (`requiredEnvAnyOf`), V3 capped at one 60 s request. Docs: go-live.md §11.1 (which Hive value goes where, V2 vs V3), docs-site/go-live.html regenerated, render-deploy.md, vps-deploy.md, content-safety-miss.md, staging-gate.md, both .env.example files. Tests: hive-v3.test.ts (request shape, Bearer, mapping, policy parity, sampling bound, multipart frames, 429/403/405/5xx, failures), hive-config.test.ts, hive.test.ts V3 block, default-registry V3 selection, env-file one-of checks, live-providers, golden A4-04 (4-minute render on V3: 4 sampled frames, no callback task → READY_FOR_REVIEW).
- DECISION: default frames for long renders = 10 (10 of ~100 daily requests; a 6-minute render is then checked every 36 s, nothing between samples). Operator can raise to 60 per render. Full-coverage long-form and production volume need V2 Enterprise (`HIVE_API_VERSION=v2`).
- DECISION: when both keys are set and `HIVE_API_VERSION` is empty, V2 wins (Enterprise key preferred).
- DECISION: an organisation's own (BYOC) Hive key is still V2. With the platform on V3, a BYOC V2 key cannot scan renders over 90 s (no async path is taken) and fails closed.
- NOT VERIFIED: no live call to Hive V3 (no credentials used here). Unverified against the live API: that the Secret Key alone authenticates, that a presigned R2 URL is accepted as `media_url`, that a video counts as one request against the daily limit, the exact 429 body, and that multipart JPEG frames are accepted. Operator to run `npm run gate:live -- --only hive --confirm` with `HIVE_V3_SECRET_KEY` and a ≤ 60 s `LIVE_TEST_MEDIA_URL`.

[2026-09-30] [20.5] Production R2 buckets without a jurisdiction. The operator created the production buckets (eu1prod–eu4prod, eu-backup-prod, eu-corpus-source) in R2's default location and chose to keep them ("users will be global"); both production tokens were verified against the default endpoint (each reaches exactly its intended buckets) and refused on the `.eu.` endpoint. `setup:check` now accepts an empty R2_JURISDICTION (reminder, not missing) and eu/us/fedramp, and flags a space after `=` (compose would keep it; found in the operator's file). pgBackRest's endpoint in deploy/vps/compose.yml now omits the jurisdiction label when R2_JURISDICTION is empty (was defaulting to eu; checked with docker compose config on the server).
- DECISION: production storage is not EU-only; the legal drafts (20.4) must not promise EU-only file storage. Staging buckets stay EU.

[2026-09-30] [20.4] Legal document drafts. The six `content/legal/en-GB/*.md` placeholders are now complete UK-law (England and Wales) drafts written from the code: Terms of Service (B2B only, plans/trial/auto-renewal/VAT/top-ups, no refunds except where the law requires, cost caps and fair use, customer owns inputs/outputs, AI-output caveats, Connected Platforms, suspension, 90-day read-only then deletion, 12-month-fees liability cap, England and Wales), Privacy Policy (controller vs processor, lawful-bases table, EU (Finland) hosting, global R2 file storage and transfers, retention table, rights, ICO), Cookie Policy (the cookies Better Auth and the app actually set: `__Secure-studio.session_token` 14 days rolling, `session_data` 60 s, `two_factor` 10 min, `trust_device` 30 days, `admin_session` staff only, `studio.locale` 1 year; `studio.businessId` local storage; no analytics or advertising cookies), Acceptable Use Policy (CSAM, NCII, deepfakes, voice cloning without documented consent, elections, IP, scanning sites you do not own, platform manipulation, limit circumvention; Hive checks and enforcement), Data Processing Agreement (Art. 28, 48-hour breach notice, 30-day sub-processor notice with objection, Annex 1 processing details, Annex 2 security measures) and Sub-processors (Hetzner Helsinki, Cloudflare R2 global jurisdiction per the operator decision of 2026-09-30 that production buckets are not EU-jurisdiction because users will be global, so no text promises EU-only file storage; AWS KMS London, Resend EU, Stripe, optional Sentry; Anthropic, OpenAI, Runway, Luma, HeyGen, ElevenLabs, Shotstack, Hive, AssemblyAI EU; optional Browserless; stock libraries and HIBP receive no personal data; Connected Platforms listed as independent controllers). Operator details are seven fill-in markers (`[[COMPANY LEGAL NAME]]`, `[[COMPANY NUMBER]]`, `[[REGISTERED ADDRESS]]`, `[[ICO REGISTRATION NUMBER]]`, `[[CONTACT EMAIL]]`, `[[PRIVACY EMAIL]]`, `[[DPO OR PRIVACY LEAD]]`) listed with sources in `content/legal/FILL-IN.md`; each file starts with an unrendered HTML comment (draft date, solicitor review, fill in the markers). Gate: new `src/lib/legal/markers.ts` (browser-safe, shared with the demo): `unfilledMarkers`, `legalTextState` (placeholder / fill_in / ready); `isPlaceholder` is true for either, so terms or privacy with any `[[…]]` marker keep production sign-up closed; `legalReadiness` docs gain `state` and `unfilled`; the legal page and demo show a new "This document is a draft" banner; the Admin Centre chip says "details to fill in" with a hint; `check-ready.ts` prints `FILL-IN` with the markers; `setup:check` says "fill in the [[…]] details, see content/legal/FILL-IN.md". CI's e2e stand-in texts carry no markers, so the e2e job is unaffected. i18n: 4 keys (`legal.draft.*`, `adminOrgs.legal.fillIn`, `fillInHint`) in 11 locales, added to the review lists. Docs: go-live.md §12 (drafts exist; only markers + solicitor review remain) and docs-site/go-live.html regenerated, vps-deploy.md "Legal documents", CLAUDE.md.
- OPERATOR: fill in the seven markers, set `STUDIO_LEGAL_ENTITY_NAME` to the same company name, have a solicitor review all six texts, and confirm each provider's contracting entity, region and transfer mechanism on the Sub-processors page against its current DPA (not all could be verified here).

[2026-09-30] [20.2] Lower price list, "lower prices, fewer videos" (operator decision 2026-09-30). `catalogue.ts` `CATALOGUE_VERSION` 2026-09-30; Stripe lookup keys unchanged, amounts in `REFERENCE_PRICES_PENCE` (what `seed-stripe-test.ts` creates). New list (GBP excl. VAT): BASIC £29/mo, £290/yr, 20 short, 0 long, caps £20/mo £5/day; STANDARD £99/mo, £990/yr, 40 short, 1 long (≤180 s), caps £73/mo £15/day; PLUS £349/mo, £3,490/yr (now 10 × monthly like the others), 80 short, 4 long (≤360 s), caps £264/mo £45/day; ENTERPRISE quoted from £1,500/mo (new `ENTERPRISE_LIST_PRICE_PENCE`), unlimited fair use, caps £1,100/mo £150/day. Top-ups: 10 short BASIC £15 / STANDARD £25 / PLUS £35, 2 long STANDARD £29 / PLUS £55; cap headroom per credit 100 / 175 / 265 / 1,000 / 2,000p. Features, seats, businesses, storage, platforms, providers and the trial (STANDARD 14 days, 5 short + 1 long, £10/day, £15 total) unchanged. Computed margins (`grossMarginTypical`, `grossMarginAtCap`; Stripe 4.45 % + 20p, infra £2/£4/£8/£40; typical = 50 % of allowance at £0.90 / £1.60 + £9 / £2.40 + £18): monthly typical BASIC 56.9 %, STANDARD 54.4 %, PLUS 55.4 %; monthly at cap 19.0 % / 17.6 % / 17.6 %; annual typical 50.0 % / 46.4 % / 47.4 %; annual at cap 4.5 % / 2.2 % / 2.0 %; top-ups at worst case 27.6 % / 24.8 % / 19.3 % / 25.9 % / 22.5 %; ENTERPRISE minimum for the £1,100 cap £1,416 (15.0 %), 19.5 % at the £1,500 list price. Guard tests (`catalogue.test.ts`): monthly ≥ 50 % typical and ≥ 15 % at cap, every price positive at cap, top-ups > 15 %, each monthly cap ≥ the full allowance at typical cost (BASIC £18 ≤ £20, STANDARD £73 = £73, PLUS £264 = £264), each credit's headroom ≥ its typical cost, ENTERPRISE min-margin logic unchanged. Hard-coded Enterprise £3,950 / £3,775 replaced by `ENTERPRISE_LIST_PRICE_PENCE` and `enterpriseMinimumMonthlyPricePence` (demo billing state and admin handler, tour text via `demo/tour/price-text.ts`); demo cost caps read the catalogue; demo usage now 33 of 40 short. - DECISION: annual stays 10 × monthly on every tier; annual plans are positive but thin at the cap (2–5 %). If the operator wants more room, lower the annual discount (e.g. 11 × monthly) rather than the caps, since the caps equal the allowance at typical cost.
- DECISION: STANDARD and PLUS monthly caps equal the full allowance at typical cost exactly, so a heavier-than-typical customer (e.g. longer AI clips) can be paused before the last videos; top-up credits add headroom when that happens.
- FINDING: the ENTERPRISE default caps fall from £400/day and £3,000/month to £150 and £1,100. Corpus ingestion bills `postmind-platform` at `STUDIO_LIBRARY_PLAN_TIER=ENTERPRISE`; a 50k run at 16 slots (£150–£380/day) now needs a per-organisation cost-cap override (Admin → Organisations → Cost caps) instead of the tier default (runbooks/corpus-ingestion.md updated).

[2026-09-30] [20.3] Month-ahead auto-scheduling and calendar (operator request 2026-09-30: "auto schedule and view in calendar for up to 1 month"). Posting-plan presets (`src/lib/studio/drip-presets.ts`: "3 a week" Mon/Wed/Fri, "5 a week" Mon–Fri, "Every day"; weekdays 12:30, weekends 09:00, in the queue's time zone or the viewer's) fill the drip queue panel's slots for editing before "Save slots" (which turns the queue on; a queue that is off now says so and can be saved on), and onboarding's last step has "Plan your month" (one click saves a plan). Month-ahead view: `GET /api/studio/businesses/:id/drip-queue/upcoming?from&to` (default now → +31 days, ≤ 62 days, `studio:project:read`, organisation-scoped) returns open slots (not held by an approved video, ≥ 2 min ahead, within the 56-day horizon), held slots and the business's scheduled/publishing post count; the calendar draws open slots as dashed, non-interactive markers ("Open posting time at 12:30, no video booked yet" for screen readers; month grid in the room a cell has left, "+N open slots" beyond; phone agenda too), a legend, and "Next 30 days: N posts scheduled · M open slots" above the grid (or "The drip queue is off — set posting times", which jumps to the panel). Create: "No date — use the next free drip-queue slot" sends `publishPolicy: SCHEDULED` without a start time (before, the drip queue was reachable only through the API). Honest scheduling: a SCHEDULED project that gets no slot (queue off, no drip platform among its targets, or no free slot within 8 weeks) stores `metadata.scheduleIssue` in the approval transaction (cleared once scheduled), is audited (`studio.project.schedule_unassigned`), logged (warn) and notified to the creator (kind `auto_publish_failed`, messages `scheduleQueueOff` / `scheduleNoPlatform` / `scheduleNoFreeSlot`); the Review screen shows the reason with "Open the calendar" and "Try again" (`POST /projects/:id/auto-publish/retry` now also re-plans such a project against the current queue: `{ requeued, scheduled, unscheduled }`; `GET …/auto-publish` returns `scheduleIssue`); the automation panel now lists targets for SCHEDULED projects too. Validation: `scheduledStartAt` on project create/update ≤ 180 days ahead (`ValidationError`, shared `src/lib/studio/schedule-window.ts`, which publications and reschedule now use); Create and Review publish-panel datetime inputs carry min (now + 1 min) / max (now + 180 days) and say "too far ahead" / "in the past" before calling the API. Landing hero "A week of short videos" → "A month of short videos"; "schedule the week" / "Plan the week" copy → the month ahead (11 locales, marketing test, Playwright happy path). All new strings in 11 locales (catalogue and physical-CSS checks pass; review lists regenerated). Demo: seeded "3 a week" queue with real zoned slot maths, the upcoming route, a video waiting for a free slot ("Harvest loaf week") whose Try again schedules it, the 180-day rule on POST /projects, a Phase 20 What's new group and an "Everything built" entry. Runbook `review-publish-automation.md` updated. Tests: unit (presets, schedule window and client bounds, upcoming window/open slots/reason, constants pinned: `MAX_DRIP_SLOTS` = 28, `DRIP_HORIZON_DAYS` = 56 ≥ 31, `readScheduleIssue`, create body, month grouping), components (calendar markers/summary/presets/save refresh, queue-off jump, Review notice + Try again, publish-panel bounds, posting-plan card), DB integration `test/api/p20-schedule-month.test.ts` (upcoming auth/403/window cap/tenant scope/held vs open/scheduled count; queue-off → notice, audit, notification, retry; wrong-platform and full-horizon reasons), plus POST /publications 181 days → 400 and project create/update scheduledStartAt 181/200 days → 400.
- DECISION: open slots are only listed up to the 56-day horizon (now + DRIP_HORIZON_DAYS): no approval can take a slot beyond it yet, so showing it as "open" would overpromise. The calendar still navigates without limit.
- DECISION: preset times (weekdays 12:30, weekends 09:00) are a neutral default, not a best-time claim; 15.A6 best-time suggestions stay advisory and separate. A plan replaces the draft slots (it does not merge) and keeps the queue's existing time zone and platforms.
- DECISION: the "no slot" notification reuses kind `auto_publish_failed` (same preference switch and email opt-in as other auto-publish problems) with its own message keys, rather than adding a notification kind (no preferences or catalogue migration).
- DECISION: "Try again" re-plans only an APPROVED SCHEDULED project whose latest approval wrote no outbox rows; anything else keeps the 13.21 behaviour (re-arm FAILED rows).
- DECISION: `scheduledStartAt` keeps accepting past times (planSchedule publishes those as soon as approved); only the 180-day upper bound is new.
- NOT VERIFIED: the demo page in a real browser (demo build and demo tests pass; the preview pane here serves only the main checkout).

## Phase 19

[2026-09-29] [19.3] Rate limits beyond IP (Track 3): `src/lib/auth/account-rate-limits.ts`, enforced in Better Auth's before-hook (`config.ts`), on the existing Valkey store. 2FA verify (`/two-factor/verify-totp|verify-backup-code|verify-otp`): 5 per 10 minutes per account, keyed by the session's user or, mid sign-in, the user the signed `two_factor` cookie points at; on top of the per-IP rule and Better Auth's own lockout (10 consecutive failures, 15 minutes). Invites (`/organization/invite-member`, resends included): 20/hour per organisation (charged only when the inviter is a member, so a stranger cannot use up an organisation's budget) and 30/hour per inviter across organisations. The hook also runs for server-side `auth.api` calls (`dispatchAuthEndpoint` in better-auth 1.7.6), so Studio's own invite route is covered; its gateway maps the 429 to `RateLimitError` (429 + Retry-After, `reason: invite_rate_limited`). Password reset and verification resend were already limited per address (3 per 15 minutes). All answer the same 429 `RATE_LIMITED` whatever the account state. Tests: unit (`account-rate-limits.test.ts`, gateway mapping) and DB integration against rotating IPs (`test/integration/auth-flows.test.ts`: 2FA blocks the 6th attempt even with the right code from a new IP; invite 21st refused, strangers not charged, server-side call refused; resend limit identical for known and unknown addresses). runbooks/auth.md updated.

Tests: `legal-links.test.ts`, `marketing.test.tsx`.

Installed with `npm install --force` because npm 12's resolver trips on better-auth's optional peer `@sveltejs/kit` (vite 8 vs vitest's vite 7); `--legacy-peer-deps` was rejected because it dropped installed peers (@testing-library/dom, webpack) from the lockfile. `npm ci` on the resulting lockfile is clean.

Fields checked against `getSchema()` of the pinned version with the organization, twoFactor and admin plugins (2026-09-29). The auth tables have FKs among themselves only.
- VERIFIED (docs read 2026-09-29): better-auth.com/docs/adapters/prisma, /docs/integrations/next, /docs/concepts/rate-limit, /docs/concepts/session-management, /docs/plugins/2fa, /docs/authentication/email-password, plus the pinned package's type definitions. Differences from the plan's memory-based §2.1: X-Forwarded-For chains are only trusted with `advanced.ipAddress.trustedProxies`; rate-limit custom storage is a single atomic `consume(key, rule)`; the 2FA TOTP secret is encrypted with the auth secret, backup codes are plain JSON unless `storeBackupCodes: 'encrypted'` (the plan's "hashed" is not possible: the library compares the decrypted list); 2FA has built-in lockout; `requireEmailVerification` already answers sign-up for an existing email with the same 200 and calls `onExistingUserSignUp`; `account.accountLinking.requireLocalEmailVerified` (default true) refuses to link an OAuth identity into an unverified local account; `verification.storeIdentifier: 'hashed'` exists; `disabledPaths`, `trustedOrigins`, `cookiePrefix`, `cookieCache`, `membershipLimit`, `organizationHooks`, `databaseHooks`, `trustedProviders` exist as named in the plan.

[2026-09-29] [18.B] Track B email (Resend, plans/phase-18.md §2.8): `studio.email_outbox` + `send-email` job on a new `studio-email` queue (5 retries, Resend `Idempotency-Key` = the row's key, 429/5xx/network retry, anything else fails at once; one-time links redacted after send; kill-switch exempt) + a 5-minute sweeper (re-enqueues lost rows, purges finished rows after 90 days); `AuthMailer` production implementation (`src/lib/email/mailer.ts`, wired as `ApiDeps.mailer`; console transport outside resend mode); 20 templates (`src/emails/*`: auth, organisation, billing, account + one generic `notification` template rendering the 16.5 keyed message) in all 11 locales under `email.*`, HTML + text, `dir="rtl"` for ar, params validated at queue and render time (http(s) links only, ISO dates formatted per locale), en-GB fallback; `ResendEmailSender` for notifications (addresses from `studio.users`, drops unverified / deleted / banned, one row per recipient, per-user locale) wired into `notifierFor` and the pipeline notifier; Svix-verified `POST /api/email/resend/webhook` (5-minute tolerance, 400 on a bad signature, per-IP `webhookRateLimiter`) — permanent bounces, complaints and Resend-suppressed addresses go to `email_suppressions` (SHA-256 of the lowercased address) and are audited without the address; one-click unsubscribe `GET|POST /api/email/unsubscribe` (HMAC token over user + org + kind with `STUDIO_UNSUBSCRIBE_SECRET`, RFC 8058 headers, GET only shows a localised confirm page); preferences API `emailDelivery` now `active | suppressed | pending_setup` and the dialog says "we can't email you"; `scripts/email/unsuppress.ts`; runbooks/email-resend.md (new) and notifications-email.md (option B chosen; GAP removed); "Not built" entry now "first real send on staging". Rendering happens only in the worker.
- DECISION (transport): `fetch` to `POST https://api.resend.com/emails` instead of the `resend` SDK (one endpoint). Webhook signatures verified with node:crypto (Svix algorithm) instead of relying on `standardwebhooks`, which is only a transitive dependency of @anthropic-ai/sdk here.
- DECISION (queue): a dedicated `studio-email` queue (default concurrency 5; VPS 2) so verification / reset mail never waits behind publishing; added to the worker's default queue list in deploy/vps/compose.yml.
