## Deployment: single VPS (Hetzner)

[2026-09-29] [VPS] Studio deployable on ONE Hetzner Cloud server (Germany) with Docker Compose; runbooks/vps-deploy.md is now the primary deployment path. Docs read 2026-09-29 and cited inline (Hetzner server/firewall/backups docs, FAQ and Cloud API `change_type`; Docker Engine on Ubuntu, Docker + UFW, logging, Compose interpolation; Caddy automatic HTTPS/import/trusted_proxies/lb_try_duration; Cloudflare Full (strict), proxy status, Always Use HTTPS; pgBackRest user guide/configuration/command reference/release notes; PostgreSQL 17 archiving and resource docs; BullMQ going-to-production; Valkey persistence/admin; GitHub GHCR + artifact attestations; tsx Node CLI; Prisma 6 migrate deploy).
- DECISION: Render dropped as the primary path for cost (about $536/month for staging + production); `render.yaml` and `scripts/render/*` are kept as an alternative and still validated in CI (render-deploy.md marked ALTERNATIVE).
- DECISION (size, lead 2026-09-29): default target Hetzner CPX12 (1 shared vCPU, 2 GB, 40 GB). Default-on services fit in 1,760 MB of limits (Postgres 384, Valkey 128, web 448, worker 704, Caddy 96; test asserts ≤ 1,800 MB) + 2 GB swap; every CPU limit ≤ 1. Monitoring (profile `monitoring`), browserless (profile `headless-render`) and always-on staging are opt-in and documented as needing 4 GB / 2 vCPU. The runbook covers what fits, the signs of outgrowing it, and resizing in place while keeping the disk (so it can scale back down).
- `deploy/vps/compose.yml` (one project per environment: `postmind-studio`, `postmind-studio-staging`): web (`node …/next start`, V8 heap cap), ONE worker (`node --import tsx scripts/worker.ts` + all six queues, configurable via `STUDIO_WORKER_QUEUES`; healthcheck on the metrics listener), Postgres 17 + pgvector 0.8.6 (the CI image) + pgBackRest (local build `deploy/vps/postgres/`), Valkey 8.1.10 (AOF everysec + RDB, `noeviction`, maxmemory), one-shot `migrate`, `ops`/`backup-storage` (profile ops), `postgres-restore` (profile restore). DATABASE_URL/REDIS_URL/APP_URL/redirect URIs are derived in compose from the env file. Nothing but Caddy publishes public ports; Prometheus/Alertmanager bind 127.0.0.1. Log rotation, restart policies, healthchecks and limits on every service.
- `deploy/vps/compose.edge.yml` + `caddy/`: one Caddy 2.11.4 for both environments (automatic HTTPS, `studio-edge` network, one generated site file per environment, `/api/metrics` 404 at the edge, `/api/studio/internal/**` limited to `STUDIO_INTERNAL_ALLOWED_CIDRS` — closed when empty, `trusted_proxies` for Cloudflare proxied mode, `lb_try_duration 30s` across web restarts).
- DECISION (backups): pgBackRest, not WAL-G — WAL-G issue #2550 (wal-push hangs against R2) is open; pgBackRest's R2 429 problem was fixed in 2.57.0 and it installs from the PGDG repo the postgres image already configures. WAL archiving (`archive_timeout = 60`) + nightly full (Sunday)/diff backups to `postgres/<env>/` in the R2 backup bucket, client-side aes-256-cbc, time retention 22 days (weekly fulls → everything ≤ 30 days; deploy.sh refuses > 22). Backup credentials live in a separate `<env>.backup.env` passed only to postgres and backup-storage (interpolation only), never to web/worker.
- `scripts/vps/`: `deploy.sh <sha>` (preflight of the env files → pull → build Postgres → datastores → pgBackRest stanza-create + check → migrate + seed → web → worker (`--workers-first`) → profiles → Caddy site + reload → readiness inside and through Caddy → tag history; `--previous` = rollback, `--ssh user@host` runs it on the server so it works as STAGING_DEPLOY_CMD, `--env staging`, `--stop`; keeps the last 3 images), `compose.sh`, `backup.sh` (db/storage/info, alerts on failure), `pg-restore.sh` (snapshot → drill into postgres-restore → staging-gate `--restore-check` → cleanup; `in-place --yes` for incidents), `healthcheck.sh` (readiness via Caddy, container health/OOM, swap/memory/disk, queue backlog > 500 → `OPS_ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL` or Alertmanager, deduplicated), `install-timers.sh` (systemd timers: backups 02:30 UTC, health every 5 min), `ci-fixture.sh`.
- `deploy/vps/bootstrap.sh` (Ubuntu 24.04, idempotent, no secrets): deploy user with root's SSH keys + passwordless sudo, sshd drop-in (no root, no passwords) validated with `sshd -t`, UFW 22 (limit)/80/443 tcp+udp, fail2ban sshd jail, unattended-upgrades, Docker from Docker's apt repo (deb822 + docker.asc), 2 GB swap, swappiness 10, overcommit_memory 1 (Valkey), UTC, `/etc/postmind-studio`, `/var/lib/postmind-studio`, `/opt/postmind-studio`.
- Code: `STUDIO_FFMPEG_MAX_CONCURRENT` (src/lib/studio/pipeline/process-limit.ts): a per-process FIFO cap on ffmpeg/ffprobe children, applied in media-probe `run()` (every FFmpeg call goes through it); unset = unchanged behaviour; the VPS sets 1. Tests with real child processes.
- CI: the `docker` job also runs the VPS stack smoke (Postgres build, Valkey config, migrate, web readiness, `node --import tsx` worker entry); new `vps-config` job (shellcheck, `docker compose config` for every profile + edge, `caddy validate` on rendered sites, `promtool check config`); new `publish-image` job on `main` (GHCR push tagged with the SHA, BuildKit provenance mode=max, `actions/attest@v4` pushed to the registry). staging-gate.yml installs an SSH key (`VPS_SSH_PRIVATE_KEY`, `VPS_SSH_KNOWN_HOSTS`) for the VPS deploy command.
- Tests: test/unit/vps-compose.test.ts (44): pinned images (Postgres base = the CI pgvector tag), no host ports for Postgres/Redis/web/worker, only Caddy on 80/443, Redis noeviction + AOF + RDB + DB 3, pgBackRest archiving/encryption/retention ≤ 30 days, backup secrets never reach web/worker, pool sizes fit max_connections, worker covers every queue in QUEUES, migrate before rollout, healthchecks/restart/log rotation/limits everywhere, memory sum ≤ 1.8 GB and CPU ≤ 1 by default, heap caps, FFmpeg = 1, every requireEnv provided by compose or REQUIRED in deploy/vps/.env.example, every interpolated variable documented, no literal secrets, tag rule = staging-gate IMAGE_TAG, staging project separation. requiredAtStartup moved to test/helpers/required-env.ts (shared with render-blueprint.test.ts).
- Runbooks: new vps-deploy.md; deploy.md, staging-gate.md, rollback.md, monitoring-deploy.md, backup-recovery.md, runbooks/README.md, README.md and render-deploy.md updated so the VPS is the primary path.
- UNVERIFIED (no Docker, shellcheck or server here): every script and compose file has only been parsed (bash -n, js-yaml, the tests). CI's `docker` / `vps-config` jobs are the first real run of the compose stack, shellcheck, Caddy and promtool; the first server deploy is the first run of bootstrap.sh, Caddy's certificate, pgBackRest against R2 (stanza-create/check/backup/restore), the systemd timers and the health check. That `docker compose` enforces `deploy.resources.limits` is checked on the server with `docker stats` (runbook step 7). Whether 2 GB is enough under real load is to be watched with the health check.
- RISK: on 2 GB the migration step runs next to the old release and leans on swap; a large render can exceed the worker's 704 MB limit (it may use swap up to the same amount); the monitoring stack is off, so alerting is the 5-minute health check, Sentry and an external uptime check until the server has 4 GB.

## Phase 17

[2026-09-28] [17.1] Abandoned uploads: the daily `sweep-abandoned-uploads` job (14.2) now takes its grace period from `STUDIO_UPLOAD_ABANDON_GRACE_HOURS` (default 24, whole hours 1–720), never touches a PENDING upload that a project (UPLOAD sourceRef / claimed projectId), a video asset or slide (same object), or a brand kit (logo/watermark/cards, `upload:<id>` fonts) references (logged instead), claims the row PENDING → FAILED before deleting the object through the storage layer (S3/R2) and puts it back if the delete fails, and pages with keyset pagination so skipped rows are not re-read. deploy.md GAP closed; `.env.example` documents the setting.
[2026-09-28] [17.2] Lost publish jobs: `redrive-lost-publications` (every 10 minutes, studio-publish; services/lost-publications.ts) re-enqueues SCHEDULED publications overdue by more than `STUDIO_LOST_PUBLISH_MARGIN_MINUTES` (default 15) whose own jobs are not queued (fire-scheduled, publish-video attempt id, YouTube quota id; new optional `JobQueue.jobState`, BullMQ `getJob`/`getState`), once, under `publish-video__<id>__redrive__<retryCount>__<due ms>`; a still-PENDING schedule is marked FIRED first. Skips kill-switched (workspace/project/platform), deleted-project and unknown-outcome publications. Audit `studio.publication.lost_job_redriven` + warn log. review-publish-automation.md GAP closed.
[2026-09-28] [17.3] Account-status check: `check-platform-accounts` (hourly at :20, studio-analytics) checks each active connection once per `STUDIO_ACCOUNT_CHECK_INTERVAL_HOURS` (24), ≤ `_BATCH` (100) per run, `_SPACING_MS` (2000) apart, with the documented cheap reads (TikTok user/info, YouTube channels.list mine, X users/me, LinkedIn userinfo — the existing OAuth clients after getAccessToken; Facebook `/me` with the Page token, Instagram content_publishing_limit; docs cited in services/account-status.ts). Only an auth refusal flips the state to needs_reconnect, with an in-app notification (new kind `connection_needs_reconnect`, keys `connectionNeedsReconnect` / `metaConnectionNeedsReconnect`, 11 locales) and audit `studio.connection.needs_reconnect`; transient errors are recorded as `unreachable`, a 429 pauses that platform for the run. Expand-only migration `20261003040000_connection_status_check` (`statusCheckedAt`, `statusCheckOutcome`, index); Connections shows "Access checked <date>" (11 locales). platform-account-revocation.md GAP closed (Core ones kept).
- DECISION (Meta): Studio holds no Meta app token (Core owns the app), so no `debug_token`; the check uses reads Studio already depends on, and expiry / Graph 190 as publishing does.
[2026-09-28] [17.4] Provider-outage alerts: the router now counts `studio_provider_passed_over_total{provider,reason}` (run-time skips only) and `studio_provider_selected_total{provider}`; new warning `StudioProviderFailoverRateHigh` (> 20% of routings reaching a provider pass it over for circuit_open / too_slow / no_cost_estimate, ≥ 10 in 15 min, for 10 min) next to the existing breaker-open > 5 min warning; promtool tests for both. provider-outage.md "Alerts" section replaces the GAP; runbooks/README.md series and tests updated.
[2026-09-28] [17.5] Object-storage backup copy: scripts/ops/backup-storage.ts (logic src/lib/studio/ops/storage-backup.ts + storage-backup-run.ts) copies new/changed objects of the assets, renders and thumbnails buckets (library with --only) into ONE backup bucket `S3_BACKUP_BUCKET` under `assets/` `renders/` `thumbnails/` — server-side CopyObject when the backup is behind the same endpoint, streamed GET → PUT otherwise (`S3_BACKUP_REGION`), clients from the storage factory (S3 and R2). Incremental by listing both sides (size + ETag, or copy newer than source for multipart ETags). Deleted sources get a tombstone in `.studio-backup/state.json`; copies are deleted `S3_BACKUP_RETENTION_DAYS` (default = max = 30) after first seen missing, so an organisation hard delete / purge ages out of the backup within 30 days + 1 run (purge-storage.ts does not touch the backup: the app token has no access to it). Mass-tombstone valve (> 25 % and ≥ 100, or an empty source listing) stops a bucket unless --allow-mass-tombstone; corrupt state fails instead of resetting clocks. Dry run by default, --apply; JSON log lines `storage_backup_bucket` / `storage_backup_run`, exit 1 on any error. Own key pair `S3_BACKUP_ACCESS_KEY_ID/SECRET`. render.yaml: cron `studio-backup-storage-<env>` (docker, 03:30 UTC, no DB wrapper, config + secrets groups, backup bucket/token `sync: false`; staging 0.5c-512mb checksPass, production 1c-2g off). backup-recovery.md GAP closed; r2-setup.md step 7 (backup bucket + token), render-deploy.md, .env.example.
- DECISION: one backup bucket with prefixes (one bucket, one token scope, restore = copy `<logical>/<key>` → `<key>`); retention capped at 30 days so the GDPR statement cannot be configured away.
[2026-09-28] [17.6] S3 presigned PUT: the signer now signs `content-type` on S3 as on R2 (`X-Amz-SignedHeaders=content-type;host`), and URLs are signed by `createPresignS3Client()` (storage-client.ts, requestChecksumCalculation WHEN_REQUIRED) so they carry no `x-amz-checksum-crc32=AAAAAA==` / `x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm`. Evidence: AWS SDK maintainer (aws/aws-sdk#480) — S3 ignores checksums in the query string; S3 docs — a mismatching checksum fails with BadDigest; SDK #6810 — WHEN_REQUIRED is the switch. Server-side PutObject/UploadPart keep the SDK default. signer.test.ts + deploy.md updated.
[2026-09-28] [17.7] CI: database job on `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.6-pg17` (Render Postgres 17); docker job builds ops/render/monitoring/Dockerfile and checks its binaries and rules; verify job validates render.yaml against Render's Blueprint JSON Schema (https://render.com/schema/render.yaml.json, the SchemaStore "Render Blueprints" entry) via scripts/render/validate-blueprint.ts — vendored byte-identical at ops/render/render.yaml.schema.json, sha256-pinned, `--check-upstream` reports drift; ajv 8 (Ajv2020, already installed transitively via schema-utils and shadcn) added as an explicit devDependency. Also run in npm test (src/lib/render/blueprint-schema.test.ts).
[2026-09-28] [17.8] Ownership statement recorded as `{locale, messageKey, text}`: POST /businesses/:id/scan-website takes `ownershipStatement: { locale, messageKey: 'business.scan.ownershipStatement', text? }`; services/approved-statements.ts loads the approved text from messages/<locale>.json (src/lib/i18n loadMessages) and stores it (website_scans.ownershipStatement + new ownershipStatementLocale / ownershipStatementKey), refusing a text that is not the approved wording for that locale (400 "does not match an approved version", NFC/whitespace-insensitive). A plain string (Phase 15/16 wire format) is accepted only when it equals an approved text in some locale (that locale is recorded). Audit `studio.website_scan.start` carries all three. Voice consent: the dialog also sends consentStatementLocale (+ consentStatementKey when the suggested wording was kept); stored on voice_profiles and in the audit entry (the recording stays the evidence). Migration 20261003030000 (expand-only: 4 nullable columns + video_projects.name DROP NOT NULL); older rows keep NULL. runbooks/scan-blocked.md updated.
- DECISION: "approved versions" = the texts in the catalogues shipped with the build; a wording change is a reviewed catalogue change and earlier rows keep the text agreed to at the time (no separate version registry).
[2026-09-28] [17.9] Server-originated text in the reader's language. (1) Failure reasons stay `<code>: <detail>`; src/lib/client/failure-reasons.ts parses the code (kill-switch level, provider/platform + error class, failed shots, failed checks, scan cap, wrapped causes like `planning_failed: kill_switch_workspace`) and components/studio/failure-reason.tsx renders `failures.codes.*` with only non-Studio text (provider message, checker reason, reviewer note) shown untranslated in a `<bdi dir=auto>`; unknown reasons show as stored. Used on the review screen, shot panel, both publication lists, the export list and scan errors. The five fixed English sentences the server stored without a code now carry one (`robots_blocked`, `no_pages`, `scan_cost_cap`, `scan_images_capped`, `ownership_disputed`, `youtube_quota_deferred`); older rows are recognised by their exact sentence. (2) Quality checks carry `detailKey` + `detailParams` (33 keys, quality-checks.ts / quality-sync.ts / force-approve / T&S allow); the quality panel renders review.quality.details.* (numbers in the locale), stored English as the fallback; labels for audio_sync, caption_sync, watermark, brand_kit, force_approved added. Auto-approval "needs review" reasons carry `params` and render from review.automation.reasons.<code>. (3) "Untitled video" is never stored: video_projects.name is nullable, create/duplicate store null (and the legacy placeholder an older client sends), PATCH name:null clears; src/lib/project-name.ts gives server English ("Untitled video") and message params ('' → the UI's common.untitledVideo); every reader updated (notifications, cost alerts, safety reviews, auto-publish outbox, share comments, calendar shadow title, ideation prompt gets no name instead of the placeholder, lists, review header, calendar, onboarding, share preview, admin panels). Captions never come from the project name (checked: publish panel uses caption suggestions / brief hook; demo mock fixed). (4) Template categories: shared useTemplateCategory() (templates screen + slideshow picker) with the full list incl. listicle, listicle_5, listicle_10. 97 keys × 11 catalogues (Arabic six plural forms), review lists regenerated. Render tests in ar + zh-Hans (server-text.i18n.test.tsx, server-text-names.i18n.test.tsx).
- DECISION: failure reasons are parsed in the UI rather than adding `failure{code,params}` to every API response: the stored values already lead with a stable code, so one pure parser covers projects, shots, publications, exports and scans, including historic rows. The admin safety-review queue still shows the reviewer-facing English `reason` (staff view; mostly checker text).

## Deployment: Render

[2026-09-28] fix: narration captions (13.6) now use the business default brand kit, not only a kit chosen on the project (same resolver as composition, pipeline/brand-resolve.ts). Found by a flaky GP-10: it read the first overlay without an order; it now checks every overlay on the first shot.
[2026-09-28] [Render] Studio deployable on Render from one Blueprint (`render.yaml`): project `postmind-studio`, environments staging (auto-deploy after CI) and production (manual promotion), everything in frankfurt. Render docs read 2026-09-28 and cited inline (blueprint-spec, private-network, deploys, rollbacks, postgresql-backups/-extensions/-connection-pooling, key-value, compute plans, pricing, Cloudflare DNS, REST API deploys/rollback).
- Per environment: Postgres 17 (pgvector, private only), Key Value (Valkey 8, noeviction, journal+snapshot, `ipAllowList: []`), web (Docker, preDeploy = `prisma migrate deploy` + idempotent seed, health `/api/health/ready`), six queue workers, and a monitoring service. Headless render (browserless) is left out: Render's private network is flat, so compose's isolated `headless` network cannot be reproduced (runbook "Not on Render").
- DECISION (worker type): queue workers are private services (`pserv`), not `worker`. Background workers cannot receive private-network traffic, so Prometheus could not scrape `:9464/metrics`. A pserv must bind a port, and the metrics listener does, because `METRICS_TOKEN` is always set.
- DECISION (secrets): non-secret config lives in `studio-config-<env>`. `METRICS_TOKEN` is `generateValue` in `studio-metrics-<env>` (web, workers, Prometheus). `STUDIO_INTERNAL_SERVICE_TOKEN` is `generateValue` on web; Core copies it. `studio-secrets-<env>` is left empty in the Blueprint, and the operator pastes the secrets once per environment. Groups cannot hold `sync: false`, and per-service prompts would mean pasting each secret 7 times. The only `sync: false` values are the PagerDuty key and Slack webhook on the monitoring service. `POSTMIND_SERVICE_TOKEN` is issued by Core, so it is never generated.
- DECISION (DB URL): the Blueprint sets `RENDER_POSTGRES_URL` (Render's internal connectionString); `scripts/render/with-db-url.sh` (+ `src/lib/render/database-url.ts`) builds DATABASE_URL (`schema=studio`, per-service `connection_limit`, `pool_timeout`, `application_name`=RENDER_SERVICE_NAME) and exec's web / worker / preDeploy, never logging the URL. PgBouncer off: Prisma Migrate needs a direct connection (Prisma docs), Studio only uses transaction-level advisory locks, and the connection budget fits (production Postgres 2c-8g = 200 connections).
- DECISION (monitoring): `studio-monitoring-<env>` pserv built from `ops/render/monitoring/` (Prometheus 2.55.1 + Alertmanager 0.28.1 + blackbox 0.28.0, the CI-validated versions) with the unchanged alert/SLO rules and routing; scrapes `<host>-discovery` DNS names (every instance); secrets written to files by the entrypoint. SLO rules are now loaded too (the compose config loads only studio-alerts.yml).
- `scripts/render/deploy.ts` (+ `src/lib/render/deploy.ts`): deploy a commit to a list of services via the Render API. It rolls back to a retained build when one exists, otherwise it builds the commit, deploying web first and then the rest, and waits for `live`. Used as STAGING_DEPLOY_CMD for the rollback rehearsal and for production promotion.
- Cost (list prices): staging ≈ $124/mo, production ≈ $387/mo, Pro workspace $25/mo.
- Runbooks: new render-deploy.md (account, Blueprint, secrets table, domains + Cloudflare DNS + disconnect the `postmindstudio` Workers build, first-deploy checks, promotion, rollback, scaling, PITR). deploy.md, staging-gate.md, backup-recovery.md, monitoring-deploy.md, rollback.md and README updated. `.env.example` gains RENDER_POSTGRES_URL, STUDIO_DB_CONNECTION_LIMIT, STUDIO_DB_POOL_TIMEOUT. `js-yaml` (already in the lockfile) is now a direct devDependency for the Blueprint test.
- Tests: test/unit/render-blueprint.test.ts covers:
  - frankfurt everywhere and unique names;
  - Key Value noeviction, `ipAllowList: []` and persistence;
  - Postgres pinned to PG ≥ 13;
  - a pserv per queue in both environments;
  - preDeploy runs migrate + seed;
  - every requireEnv in src is provided by render.yaml or the runbook table;
  - no literal secrets;
  - monitoring template and hosts.

  Also src/lib/render/database-url.test.ts and src/lib/render/deploy.test.ts.
- UNVERIFIED (needs a Render account): a Blueprint sync (`render blueprints validate`); Redis DB 3 (SELECT) on Render Key Value; the monitoring image build (no Docker locally); `dockerCommand` keeping the image's tini ENTRYPOINT; that dashboard-added group vars survive syncs (documented in Render's blueprint spec; runbook step 7.8 checks it).
- RISK: `/api/studio/internal/**` and `/api/metrics` are on the public web service (Engagement expects private ingress). Both are token-protected; the runbook adds a Cloudflare WAF rule and turns off the onrender.com subdomain. Render's IP allow-list for web services needs the Scale plan.

[2026-09-28] [R2] Cloudflare R2 as an alternative object store; AWS S3 stays the default and is unchanged. KMS stays on AWS (KMS_KEY_ID, envelope.ts untouched).
- Config: STORAGE_PROVIDER=s3|r2 (default s3), R2_ACCOUNT_ID, R2_JURISDICTION (eu|us|fedramp, optional), R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID, R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY. Validated with zod in src/lib/studio/storage-client.ts, which fails fast with a ConfigurationError naming every problem. There was no central env schema; each module validates its own env.
- Client factory: createStorageS3Client() / storageProvider() in storage-client.ts. It replaces the object-storage `new S3Client({ region: AWS_REGION })` in storage.ts (primary and failover), uploads/signer.ts and scripts/ops/apply-s3-lifecycle.ts. ingest-corpus and the staging-gate probes go through getAssetStorage.
- R2 client settings: region auto; endpoint https://<id>[.<jurisdiction>].r2.cloudflarestorage.com; request/response checksums WHEN_REQUIRED.
- Key layout (DECISION): provider outputs go under intermediates/orgs/<org>/projects/<p>/providers/... on R2 only. S3 keeps orgs/... plus the studio-object tag, byte-identical.
  - R2 never sends Tagging.
  - orgPrefixes() makes the organisation hard delete, its dry run and countKeysOutsidePrefix cover both prefixes on R2.
  - Readers use the stored s3Key, so existing keys keep working.
- Lifecycle: infra/r2-lifecycle.json has intermediates/ 30 d (assets), library/staging/ 2 d, and abort multipart 7 d on every bucket. There are no noncurrent-version rules and no tag filters.
  - s3-lifecycle.ts validates per provider (r2RuleProblems plus the R2 policy).
  - apply-s3-lifecycle.ts picks the file by STORAGE_PROVIDER (still dry run by default, --apply to write).
  - CI checks both files.
- CDN: CDN_URL with STORAGE_PROVIDER=r2 is a ConfigurationError. On R2, presigned GET and PUT expiry is capped at 604,800 s.
- Failover: same config shape. On R2, S3_FALLBACK_REGION is "auto" or a jurisdiction.
- Upload signer on R2 signs content-type (signableHeaders) and sends no checksum params.
- Staging gate:
  - The corpus pre-flight gains a "Storage provider" check.
  - The openai-image live test asks for the R2 env on R2.
  - The staging-gate workflow gains vars STORAGE_PROVIDER / R2_ACCOUNT_ID / R2_JURISDICTION and secrets R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID / R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY.
- Runbooks: new r2-setup.md; storage-cost, storage-failover, backup-recovery (no R2 versioning: a backup copy with ≤30 d retention), staging-gate, deploy and README updated.
- Tests: storage-client.test.ts, storage-r2.test.ts, uploads/signer.test.ts, s3-lifecycle.test.ts (R2 file), purge-storage.test.ts, organisation-hard-delete.test.ts (R2 purge), corpus-preflight.test.ts, live-providers.test.ts.
- FINDING (S3, not changed): with the installed @aws-sdk 3.1141, S3 presigned PUTs sign only `host` (the presigner treats content-type as unsignable), so Content-Type is not enforced on S3. They also carry x-amz-checksum-crc32=AAAAAA== (the CRC32 of an empty body) and x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm=CRC32. Verify browser uploads on S3 staging; if S3 rejects the checksum, apply the same WHEN_REQUIRED fix to S3 (an operator call, since S3 was to stay unchanged).
- UNVERIFIED (needs an R2 account):
  - whether R2 accepts the CRC32 checksum the SDK always sends on DeleteObjects and PutBucketLifecycleConfiguration (the runbook gives a dashboard fallback for lifecycle);
  - virtual-hosted URLs on the EU jurisdiction endpoint.

[2026-09-28] [15.INT] Integration of tracks A–E + Phase 14 merge. (1) Core kit: the three Phase 15 internal routes are in internal-contract.ts from their route zod schemas (purgeBusiness ← businessPurgeInput, attributeConversation ← attributeConversationInput + publicationIdParam, createProjectFromContent ← fromContentInput, with response schemas) → integrations/core/openapi.json regenerated; the copyable client gains purgeBusiness / attributeConversation / createProjectFromContent (types checked against the schemas); contract suite 12 → 15 checks (business purge twice, attribution 404 for a foreign publication, from-content 501 not_implemented — documented "do not wire until Core ships GET /api/internal/content/:id", Core dependency 4 in the README). (2) Purge coverage, DECISION: every organisation table is purged incl. data_exports, publication_conversations, share_links + share_link_comments, usage_events, calendar_shadows, drip_queues and provider_credentials; kept only organisation_purges + business_purges (tombstones) and takedown_requests (legal / transparency record) whose requester and publicationId the hard delete nulls (purge-tables ANONYMISE_STEPS, summary "takedown_requests:anonymised"). The organisation purge now also revokes BYOC keys at once (encryptedKey / encryptedSecondaryKey null, key cache notified); purge buckets include S3_FALLBACK_BUCKET_* (15.E9) and data_exports' bucket. The business hard delete stays separate from the organisation hard delete (a business shares the org's S3 prefix and several org tables have no businessId — documented in business-hard-delete.ts) and now also deletes drip_queues. (3) Voice captions (15.A4) are a separate lane (sortOrder 50+, ids in metadata.voiceCaptions) and never burn in a line whose words the hook overlay or a TEXT_CARD already shows at that time (withoutOnScreenDuplicates); the overlays API test counts the lanes separately. (4) The 15.B6 composition cache key includes each shot's visual/voice asset ids, so a re-voiced shot always re-composes even when its object key is reused (golden CR-02). (5) Tests follow the Phase 15 rules, gates not weakened: corpus CO-01 uses a Plus tenant (TEMPLATE), the p14 scan-ownership test sends ownershipStatement (15.D8), the p14 org-purge golden configures the thumbnails bucket 15.A3 writes to; Admin PUBLISH_PLATFORMS gains instagram_feed / facebook_feed (types.test.ts pins it to catalog PLATFORMS). DECISION A10.3 / A10.4 image generation: on-demand generation (image library generate) is gated at PLUS per A10.3; the A10.4 monthly per-business cap (20/50/200/1000) is enforced wherever library generation happens — on-demand and slideshow auto-populate (which therefore still generates, within the cap, below Plus); generated IMAGE_STILL shot stills are kept in the library and count toward the cap but are governed by the project cost budget, not blocked by it (operator to confirm).

## Phase 14

[2026-09-28] [14.1] Organisation hard delete after the purge grace (fully built). Daily job hard-delete-purged-orgs (01:30 UTC, studio-orchestration; scripts/worker.ts scheduler; queue/workers/data-retention.ts) takes every organisation_purges row in soft_deleted/hard_deleting past graceUntil and (services/organisation-hard-delete.ts) deletes every object under orgs/<org>/ in S3_BUCKET_ASSETS/RENDERS/THUMBNAILS/LIBRARY and any bucket the org's rows name (storage.ts gains list() = ListObjectsV2 1,000/page and deleteMany() = DeleteObjects Quiet, per AWS API docs; keys outside the prefix are counted, never deleted), then the org's rows in all 41 org-holding studio tables in FK-safe order, 500 per batch (services/purge-tables.ts: parameterised SQL predicates, ctid batches; incl. track-3's organisation_beta, beta_feedback, safety_audit_items; image_library_queries only for business ids no other org uses; project kill-switch flags). Idempotent and resumable (progress saved after every step; failure → hardDeleteError, retried next run). Tombstone = the organisation_purges row: state hard_deleted, hardDeletedAt, hardDeleteSummary {tables, storage, keysOutsidePrefix}; audit studio.organisation.hard_delete (system:organisation-purge); the workspace kill-switch flag and the audit trail are kept. Staff undo during the grace: state = 'cancelled' (never hard-deleted; a new Core purge restarts the grace). STUDIO_PURGE_GRACE_DAYS (default 30, Engagement 14.13; 1–365) now drives graceUntil. GET /api/studio/admin/organisations/:id/purge-plan (staff, studio:admin:moderation) = dry run: purge state + due, rows per table, objects/bytes per bucket/prefix (count capped at 10k/bucket → truncated), keys outside prefix, totals. Migration 20260930010000_purge_hard_delete_scan_ownership (columns only, NO new tables). Tests: purge-tables.test.ts (every Prisma model classified; every organisationId model purged; FK order; SQL binds the id), purge-storage/organisation-hard-delete/storage unit + DB tests, test/api/p14-purge-plan.test.ts, golden test/golden/p14-org-purge.test.ts (two orgs publish → Core purge → dry run → grace passes → job → zero rows in every table + zero objects under the prefix, tombstone + audit, other org incl. a prefix-sharing id untouched, second run no-op). Runbook platform-account-revocation.md. Not covered: cloned voices at ElevenLabs (runbook step).
[2026-09-28] [14.2] S3 lifecycle rules as code (built; waiting for DevOps). infra/s3-lifecycle.json (SDK v3 PutBucketLifecycleConfiguration rule shape): assets studio-intermediates-expire-30d (tag studio-object=provider-output — S3 prefixes cannot match orgs/*/projects/*, so storage.ts put() now tags provider-output keys; needs s3:PutObjectTagging), all buckets noncurrent versions 30 d + expired delete markers and abort incomplete multipart 7 d, library library/staging/ 2 d; no current-object expiry under orgs/ and none on renders (publication lifetime + 90 d is app logic). Abandoned uploads are app logic: daily sweep-abandoned-uploads (01:45 UTC; services/upload-sweep.ts) deletes the object of uploads still PENDING a day after their URL expired and marks them FAILED; READY uploads never touched. ops/s3-lifecycle.ts validator (schema, AWS constraints: one filter element, one expiration form, no multipart-abort/delete-marker with tag filters, ≤1,000 rules; Studio policy) + dry-run diff that keeps non-studio rules; CI via npm test (s3-lifecycle.test.ts validates the committed file). scripts/ops/apply-s3-lifecycle.ts: Get/PutBucketLifecycleConfiguration, dry run by default, --apply writes then re-reads and verifies, --only, --config. POST /api/studio/scripts/:id/regenerate { instruction? ≤ 1000 } → 202 { project: { id, state: QUEUED }, runId, staleRenders }: new run whose plan-project step is Layer 2 only for that script (queue/workers/regenerate-script.ts), reusing video_briefs (no ideation call), the owner's instruction fenced as data in the prompt, same pre-generation safety gate, the script's shots + suggested overlays replaced, the other formats keep their renders (409 for SLIDESHOW/UPLOAD or without a brief; audit studio.script.regenerate). [2026-09-27] [13.16] Queue + provider health. The circuit breaker is shared through Redis (providers/circuit-breaker-redis.ts: HASH openedAt/trialStartedAt + ZSET failures per provider + SET of providers; every read-modify-write is one Lua script with times passed from the caller's clock; 5 failures anywhere open it for every process; one platform-wide half-open trial). FAIL-SAFE: any Redis error or timeout (dedicated client, no offline queue, 500 ms command timeout) falls back to an in-memory breaker in that process (the old per-process behaviour; a warning at most once a minute); outcomes are always mirrored locally. The in-memory store is kept (tests, STUDIO_CIRCUIT_BREAKER_STORE=memory); CircuitBreaker methods may now return promises and every caller awaits them (router, tracked, provider-run, metrics sampling). GET /api/studio/admin/queues (waiting incl. prioritised, active, failed, delayed, oldestWaitingSec; 502 if Redis does not answer in 3 s) and GET /api/studio/admin/providers (breaker, errorRate1h over jobs started in the last hour with client-side refusals excluded as for the breaker, jobs1h, spendTodayPence, configured, healthy); studio:admin:providers + staff. Index provider_jobs(startedAt). Admin → Queues / Providers tabs (30 s refresh). Tests: circuit-breaker-redis.test.ts (fail-safe + real Redis when REDIS_URL), services/admin-health.test.ts, test/api/admin-health.test.ts, admin/a3-panels.test.tsx.
[2026-09-27] [13.17] Content-safety review queue (spec 16.4). NEW TABLE studio.safety_reviews (+ enum SafetyReviewState; unique project + run + kind). A script-safety REVIEW verdict now stores the plan and pauses in PLANNING before any Layer 3 spend; a review-level Hive class (with nothing block-level) pauses the quality gate in QUALITY_CHECKING (renders keep their FAILED check). metadata.safetyReview marks the run; plan-project, run-quality-gate and the stuck re-drive skip a paused run. Staff and the creator are notified (kind safety_review; staff link /admin?tab=safety). GET /api/studio/admin/safety-reviews?state= (oldest first; content: signed preview of the first flagged render; script: excerpts) and POST /api/studio/admin/safety-reviews/:id/decision { decision: ALLOW|BLOCK, note } (studio:admin:moderation + staff; 409 once decided; audit studio.safety_review.decide). ALLOW script → ASSETS_QUEUED + shots enqueued; ALLOW content → the flagged checks become "Allowed by Trust & Safety review", then READY_FOR_REVIEW (or QUALITY_FAILED when another check failed); never auto-approved (a person approves). BLOCK → FAILED `<kind>_safety_blocked_by_review: <note>`. BLOCK-level results still fail at once. Admin → Safety review tab; Review screen note while paused. GA-03 (golden automation) updated: a flagged run is now paused, not QUALITY_FAILED. Tests: pipeline/safety-review.test.ts, test/api/safety-reviews.test.ts, test/golden/admin-automation.test.ts AA-01/AA-02.

Fail closed: no STUDIO_PUBLIC_CALLBACK_BASE_URL, a failed task, or no callback within HIVE_ASYNC_TIMEOUT_MIN (default 120; a delayed gate job expires the task and cancels the tracked job). Files: providers/hive.ts, providers/hive-results.ts, pipeline/content-safety-async.ts, api/hive-webhook.ts, app/api/studio/webhooks/hive/route.ts, queue/workers/run-quality-gate.ts. [2026-09-27] [13.26] Render mastering after compose (pipeline/mastering.ts): when the gate would fail the render on loudness (outside −18…−10 LUFS) or codec (not H.264 in MP4), ffmpeg loudnorm two-pass (pass 1 print_format=json; pass 2 measured_* + offset, linear=true, 48 kHz; target −14 LUFS / −1.5 dBTP / 11 LU, per ffmpeg-filters loudnorm) and/or a libx264 Baseline yuv420p +faststart re-encode (compliant video is stream-copied); the mastered file replaces the composed one in the renders bucket, is re-probed, and metadata.mastering[scriptId] records what was done. Skipped when compliant (the usual Shotstack output: one ebur128 read). Cost: no provider spend; pass 1 ≈ 1–2 s per minute of audio, video copy similar, a full 1080p re-encode ≈ real time on one core; the file is buffered in memory for upload (≈ 150–300 MB for 8 min 1080p). PipelineDeps.mastering (absent = not mastered, reported). Tests: mastering.test.ts (plan, parse, args, replace/re-probe), test/media/mastering.test.ts (real ffmpeg, CI only: quiet VP9/WebM → −14 LUFS H.264 Baseline MP4).

[2026-09-28] [DEMO] Clickable demo of the whole build: demo/ bundles the real screens (src/components/studio) into one self-contained HTML page (`node scripts/demo/build.mjs` → demo/dist/postmind-studio-demo.html; preview with `node scripts/demo/serve.mjs`). next/link and next/navigation are shimmed with a hash router; /api/studio is served in the browser by stateful sample handlers (demo/api/handlers) that mirror the real response shapes, with timed simulations for generation, publishing, scans and ingestion; sample images are canvas-drawn and sample videos are canvas clips recorded with MediaRecorder. Every route verified to render with no uncovered API calls.

## Operator decisions

[2026-09-27] [OPS-confirm] Operator confirmations (decisions 1 and 6): the auto-approve "trusted" rules (≥ 10 human-approved projects, clean run, never ENTERPRISE) and the owner/admin role for REQUIRE_APPROVAL_FROM_ROLE are CONFIRMED as built. The Core context contract (`GET /api/internal/context/:userId`) and the Engagement attribution path (`/api/engagement/internal/publications/attribute`) are CONFIRMED.
[2026-09-27] [OPS-jwt] JWT verification pinned to RS256 (operator decision 6): `jwtVerify(..., { algorithms: ['RS256'] })` in tenant.ts; a token signed ES256 by an otherwise trusted JWKS key is refused (test).
[2026-09-27] [OPS-pgvector] pgvector location unknown on the shared cluster (operator decision 6: handle both). The init migration now adds the two embedding columns in a DO block that qualifies the type with the schema the extension actually lives in (`studio` when Studio installed it, `public` when Core did); runtime raw SQL uses src/lib/studio/vector-sql.ts (extension schema looked up once per client, identifier-validated) for `::"schema".vector` casts and `OPERATOR("schema".<=>)`. test/integration/pgvector-location.test.ts runs every migration and a similarity query with the extension pre-installed in `public` and with Studio installing it (and shows an unqualified `::vector` fails in the `public` case). The init migration's SQL changed; no production database has applied it yet.

[2026-09-27] [OPS-meta] Instagram Reels + Facebook Reels publishing and metrics UNBLOCKED (operator decision 3: Meta app approval done; Core pushes tokens, mirroring Engagement handover 9.5 / 9.6 / 14.13).
- Internal endpoints (service-to-service; X-Service-Token, not JWT; bind to private ingress): `POST /api/studio/internal/channels` (Core registers an IG account / FB Page after its Meta OAuth: organisationId, platform instagram|facebook, platformAccountId (numeric Graph id), platformAccountName, accessToken, tokenExpiresAt?, scopes[], businessId?, connectedByUserId?; idempotent upsert, 201 created / 200 updated), `DELETE /api/studio/internal/channels/:id[?organisationId=]` and `DELETE /api/studio/internal/channels?organisationId=&platform=&platformAccountId=` (disconnect; token wiped; Meta rows only), `POST /api/studio/internal/tokens/refreshed` (one channel → 404 unregistered / 409 disconnected, or `{ channels: [≤100] }` → per-channel updated|not_found|revoked; a refresh re-activates needs_reconnect, never revoked). Auth: STUDIO_INTERNAL_SERVICE_TOKEN (≥32 chars; unset/short → every internal endpoint 404s), compared in constant time (SHA-256 digests + timingSafeEqual). Bodies ≤64 KB (413, new PayloadTooLargeError), zod-validated with no values echoed; rate limit STUDIO_INTERNAL_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MIN (default 600, Redis). Audit `studio.connection.meta_register|meta_reregister|meta_disconnect|meta_token_refreshed` as `system:postmind-core`; tokens never logged or returned. src/lib/studio/api/internal.ts, services/meta-channels.ts.
- Storage: studio.platform_connections, platform 'instagram'/'facebook', same envelope encryption as TikTok/YouTube (sealTokens, encryption context bound to organisation + platform). Migration 20260928010000_meta_core_channels: `businessId` becomes NULLABLE (NULL = org-wide channel; Core may not know a Studio business). No new table.
- Credentials: `unavailableMetaCredentials` replaced by `createStoredMetaCredentials` (platforms/meta-credentials.ts): org-scoped lookup by (organisationId, platform, platformAccountId), decrypts the token. Unknown/revoked/needs_reconnect or past tokenExpiresAt → PlatformError needs_reconnect (expired also flags the row). Meta error 190 (any subcode; the publisher's error mapping now checks the code before the subcode, which fixes 190+463 being misread) during publish, metrics polling or takedown → `noteCredentialFailure` flags the channel needs_reconnect. Studio never refreshes Meta tokens itself.
- Publications: IG/FB accept `connectionId` (what the UI sends) or `platformAccountId`, and the channel must be registered and active (before, any platformAccountId was accepted). Auto-publish targets accept either.
- Metrics (analytics/fetchers.ts, same MetricsFetcher interface and spec 15.2 cadence): Instagram `GET /{ig-media-id}/insights?metric=views,reach,likes,comments,shares,saved` (developers.facebook.com/docs/instagram-platform/reference/instagram-media/insights); Facebook `GET /{video-id}/video_insights?metric=fb_reels_total_plays,post_impressions_unique,post_video_view_time,post_video_likes_by_reaction_type` (developers.facebook.com/docs/graph-api/reference/video/video_insights/). Not read, and so reported unavailable: IG watch time (Meta's reference gives no unit for ig_reels_* time metrics), FB comments and shares (post_video_social_actions combines them), avg watch %. Missing metrics are listed as unavailable.

Existing IG tests (publications API, GP-03) now register the channel first.

[2026-09-27] [OPS-corpus] Library corpus UNBLOCKED by operator decision 5 (2026-09-27: no licence is required for the 50k corpus). The tooling for BACKLOG 9.2 and 9.3 is ready. 9.2 and 9.3 themselves stay OPEN until the operator runs them (runbooks/corpus-ingestion.md). What was built:
- Licence. New `LicenseScenario.NOT_REQUIRED` (migration 20260928030000_corpus_ingestion, ALTER TYPE ADD VALUE) allows both TEMPLATE and INSPIRE. `licenseSource` always records the decision: it defaults to "Operator decision 2026-09-27: no licence required for the PostMind corpus" on ingest and on PATCH. SCRAPED (INSPIRE only) and licence-expiry gating are unchanged. Admin ingest and PATCH accept NOT_REQUIRED, and the admin UI offers it.
- Run tracking. New table `video_library_ingest_runs` (studio table count +1) with enum `LibraryIngestState`. The row is keyed by the job's runId and has no FK to video_library. States: QUEUED from the admin ingest; RUNNING, then SUCCEEDED or DUPLICATE, from the worker; FAILED with the reason from the final-failure handler. Ingest resubmission returns already-ingested sources under `skipped` and re-enqueues FAILED ones under `…__retry<n>` (BullMQ keeps failed job ids). Items accept `sourceRef` and `language`, stored on the run.
- Status endpoint. `GET /api/studio/admin/library/ingest/status?windowHours&failures&runIds` returns counts by state over the window, the open backlog, completed per hour, live items, recent failures with reasons, and per-run state. The admin Library tab has an "Ingestion status" card (components/studio/admin/ingest-status.tsx) that refreshes every 30 s.
- s3:// sources. `s3://bucket/key` is read with Studio's storage client (size + readRange, 200 MB cap), but only from buckets or prefixes in `STUDIO_CORPUS_S3_BUCKETS`. With no allow-list, s3 is refused. https:// still always goes through the SSRF guard.
- Throughput. `STUDIO_LIBRARY_CONCURRENCY` (default 2, max 32) wins over WORKER_CONCURRENCY_LIBRARY. `STUDIO_LIBRARY_PLAN_TIER` (default STANDARD) sets the tier the platform org's corpus jobs run at. The full run needs ENTERPRISE, because STANDARD's £30 a day / £150 a month caps stop it (runbook "Cost").
- Tool. `scripts/ops/ingest-corpus.ts`, with pure logic in library/corpus-manifest.ts, corpus-run.ts and corpus-report.ts.
  - It reads a CSV or JSONL manifest and validates the URL scheme (https or s3), duplicate URLs and sourceRefs, category slugs against GET /library/categories, and field sizes.
  - It is a dry run by default and prints a cost and time estimate (£0.02–£0.05 per video; time from queue concurrency × workers × an ASSUMED 3 min per video, to be replaced by the sample's measured rate).
  - `--sample N [--seed]` takes a stratified sample, waits for it, and prints the review report: ok/failed counts, category distribution, failures and 10 random /library/<id> links.
  - `--apply` submits in batches of 100 or fewer, with concurrency and exponential backoff (Retry-After honoured) on 429, 408, 5xx and network errors.
  - The state file is atomic and resumable: accepted and skipped rows are never resubmitted, and rejected ones are retried. `--report` reprints the report.
- Tests: corpus-manifest.test.ts, corpus-run.test.ts, corpus-report.test.ts, corpus-source.test.ts, licence-and-source.test.ts (both modes for NOT_REQUIRED, SCRAPED gating, s3 allow-list, resubmission decision), queue/worker-host.test.ts, test/api/library-ingest-status.test.ts, test/golden/corpus.test.ts (CO-01: manifest → sample → review → searchable → TEMPLATE project → resumed full run), and admin panel UI tests.
- NOT BUILT: the runs themselves (operator: sample, review, full), a measured per-video time, streaming the source to S3 instead of buffering it, and an admin UI for resubmitting failures (resubmit with the tool, or POST the items again).

[2026-09-27] [OPS-caps] Cost cap values (operator decision 2, GBP provider spend) as defaults in code, env still overrides: org daily BASIC £10 / STANDARD £30 / PLUS £75 / ENTERPRISE £400 (1000/3000/7500/40000p), org MONTHLY £40 / £150 / £450 / £3,000 (4000/15000/45000/300000p), global daily £2,500 (250000p) — cost/caps.ts. Env rules for these three: empty/unset = default, positive integer = override, "0"/"none"/"off" = cap disabled (DECISION: "0" used to mean a zero cap for these; STUDIO_ORG_PROVIDER_DAILY_CAP_PENCE keeps no default and its old semantics). NEW monthly org cap per tier (calendar month UTC, STUDIO_ORG_MONTHLY_CAP_PENCE_<TIER>): same semantics as the daily org cap — alerts at 80%/100% once per (ORG_MONTHLY, org, YYYY-MM, threshold), pause at 100% (CostCapPausedError scope `org_monthly`, project fails `cost_cap_paused: organisation monthly cost cap reached …`; publishing never paused), org-wide notification; month-to-date = one aggregate over provider_usage by day range. Migration 20260928040000_cost_monthly_cap: enum value CostAlertScope.ORG_MONTHLY + index provider_usage(organisationId, day) (no new table). Metric series `org_monthly`, StudioCostCapReached includes it. Default per-project budget when the client sends none: £3.50 short-form / £30 long-form (cost/project-budget.ts) applied on POST /projects (incl. TEMPLATE projects, from the template's formats) and on duplicate when the source has none; explicit values win, existing rows untouched. DECISION "long-form" = any target format > 180 s (the short-form ceiling of TikTok/Reels/Shorts) or a regular `youtube` target > 60 s. UI: Create advanced options show the default as the budget placeholder + help text; project page shows "Raise budget" (PATCH costBudgetPence) when paused by the project budget, pause message/notification say how to raise it. Admin caps report: monthly caps, month-to-date top orgs, per-cap source (default / env / disabled). Tests: cost/caps.test.ts, cost/guard.test.ts (monthly), cost/project-budget.test.ts, test/api/cost-caps.test.ts, test/golden/monthly-cap.test.ts (MC-01…MC-03), admin/cost-caps-panel.test.tsx, review/budget-raise.test.tsx, create/create-options.test.tsx; GP-03 now sets an explicit budget (3 renders exceed £3.50 at harness prices). runbooks/cost-runaway.md, .env.example.
- NOT BUILT: per-organisation cap overrides (caps are per tier), email for the 80% monthly alert (no Core email API; in-app + webhook), automatic resume of paused projects at the day/month rollover.

[2026-09-27] [OPS-music] Layer 5 music (spec 5.6; 12.2; A5.4; A3.6 step 5). SPEC DRIFT: Suno has no public API (operator decision 4) → ElevenLabs Music (https://elevenlabs.io/docs/api-reference/music/compose): providers/elevenlabs-music.ts, provider `elevenlabs-music` (own breaker/kill switch/cost lines), same ELEVENLABS_API_KEY (paid plan required; "Commercial use licensing on Starter+"), model ELEVENLABS_MUSIC_MODEL (default music_v2_5). POST /v1/music, prompt + music_length_ms + force_instrumental=true, raw MP3 → assets bucket; 3 s–5 min (longer videos loop the track); $0.15/min → pence via STUDIO_USD_TO_GBP_RATE, rounded up; bad_prompt → content_policy (non-retryable), 422 → invalid_request. Router music list: elevenlabs-music, replicate, storyblocks (Suno removed). pipeline/music.ts: one instrumental track per run, sized to the longest variant, before composition, through runProvider (router → budget/cost guard → tracking), stored as a video_assets AUDIO_MUSIC row (fingerprint = prompt key) and reused when a same-prompt track at least as long exists (re-render / retry / regenerate never pays twice). pipeline/music-prompt.ts: prompt from brief tone, brand-kit toneKeywords, slideshow template musicMood, library musicEnvelope (TEMPLATE: mood/genre/energy/BPM; INSPIRE: mood/genre) — reduced to a closed vocabulary, so no artist names, lyrics or injected text reach the provider. Composition: Shotstack audio track (AudioAsset volume 0.2 under narration, 0.7 when music is the only audio, `effect: fadeOut` on the last clip; https://shotstack.io/docs/api/#tocs_audioasset), slideshows too; LUFS gate range unchanged (−18…−10). metadata.music = { status: generated | off_for_plan | failed, runId, assetId, providerId, durationSec, reused, promptKey, descriptors | reason } replaces "skipped"; Review screen shows it (review/music-status.tsx). No migration, no new table.
- DECISION (please confirm): tier gating — spec 12.4 lists no music provider for Basic → BASIC renders stay narration-only; Standard's column names none either, but 12.2's 30 s sample includes a track and the Storyblocks library pick isn't built, so STANDARD and above get a generated track. STUDIO_MUSIC_MIN_TIER (default STANDARD) moves the line.
- DECISION: music is non-fatal. Any music failure (no provider, provider error/refusal, over budget for that one call) renders the video without music, metadata.music = { status: 'failed', reason }. CostCapPausedError and KillSwitchTriggeredError still stop the run like any provider call.
- NOT BUILT: SFX (Storyblocks catalogue), MusicGen/Storyblocks music fallbacks, per-shot music ducking automation (a constant bed level is used), the "+20–25% louder" generation note in 5.6 (ElevenLabs has no loudness parameter; levels are set in the mix). Suno's £0.08/track in 12.2 → ~6p per 30 s track at $0.15/min and 0.75 FX.
- Tests: providers/elevenlabs-music.test.ts, pipeline/music-prompt.test.ts, pipeline/music.test.ts (tiers, EDL volume/fade/loop, slideshow), test/db/music.test.ts, test/golden/music.test.ts (GM-01…GM-04), review/music-status.test.tsx.

**Phase 11 security review — fixed:** unbounded metric label cardinality (HIGH: route labels came from an "id-looking" heuristic, so arbitrary URL segments — even on unauthenticated 401s — created new time series until the process ran out of memory); labels now come from the matched route's own parameters (routeLabel), bounded by the route templates. Admin endpoints (cost, kill switch, library admin) additionally require the caller's organisation to be in STUDIO_PLATFORM_ORG_IDS (MEDIUM, defence in depth against Core granting studio:admin:* outside PostMind staff; refused in production when unset). /api/metrics answers 404 for a wrong token too (LOW).

- RESOLVED 2026-09-27 by operator decision: no licence is required for the corpus (NOT_REQUIRED; see Operator decisions [OPS-corpus]). 9.2 (sample run of 100 with operator review) and 9.3 (full ingestion) remain operator tasks, and the tooling is ready: scripts/ops/ingest-corpus.ts and runbooks/corpus-ingestion.md. At ~£0.02–£0.05 per video in provider calls (Claude vision + transcription + embedding).

The visual/audio/text weights on each embedding row are informational: similarity is one cosine distance over the analysed description (which does include what Claude saw in the keyframes).
- OCR is Claude reading one keyframe per shot, not Google Cloud Vision/Tesseract per frame: text shown briefly between keyframes is missed.
- Transitions: scene detection only finds hard cuts; every blueprint transition is "cut".
- A3.6 step 3 ("prefer providers rated stylistically similar") needs provider ratings — not built. (Step 5, music from the envelope, is built: see Operator decisions — Layer 5 music.)
- Auto-categorisation places each video in one category (schema has a single categoryId), chosen by Claude from the taxonomy; the 5% human spot-check is the admin's PATCH.
- Library previews are 10-minute signed links to the low-res muted 30-second rendition; a user could still save that rendition (not the source).

- Animation fidelity: blurIn falls back to a fade (no documented blur tween); slide directions follow Shotstack's travel-direction naming (slideInLeft → "slideRight") — confirm visually. Transition speed is Shotstack's fixed normal/Fast/Slow, not the exact animationInMs.
- Karaoke timing is evenly spread across the overlay: word-level voice timing needs a transcription provider (AssemblyAI, not built).
- Slideshow slides use their built-in text rendering; per-slide text overlays need a slide→overlay link that the A7 schema doesn't have (overlays attach to shots or renders). Whole-video overlays work for slideshows.
- Whole-video overlays attach to a render and are carried to later renders of the same platform.
- Template overlayDefaults (Phase 7) name presets by intent; they are not yet applied to slides.

Layer 1 scraped (icons, trackers, social buttons, SVGs filtered; max 60), Layer 2 stock: Pexels + Storyblocks primary, Unsplash fallback (hotlink-only, per Unsplash's API terms), Layer 3 on-demand generation.

SSRF guard: http/https on 80/443 only, no userinfo, non-public IPs refused at DNS-lookup time inside the connection (no rebinding window), redirects re-validated per hop. crawl.ts: homepage → sitemap + links (prioritising about/products/shop/services) → up to 20 more pages, early stop when the site vocabulary is stable; Browserless /content fallback for JS-rendered sites when BROWSERLESS_API_KEY is set (Playwright not bundled).

**Phase 6 security review — fixed:** business-id squatting (HIGH): a tenant could claim another tenant's business id first and lock them out, because business_profiles.businessId was globally unique; uniqueness is now (organisationId, businessId) for profiles and (organisationId, businessId, fingerprint) for the image library (migration 20260927010000, DEVIATION from A7.7), so tenants can never collide. Cost/quota abuse (MEDIUM): scans capped at 3 concurrent and 25 per 24h per organisation, stock refreshes at 100 queries/hour per organisation (429 with Retry-After). Still open: stock searches are free APIs outside the pence budget ledger (kill switch still applies at job start); Pexels has no safe-search parameter, so LLM-derived queries from a hostile site could pull unwanted stock images into a library (mitigated only by the user-editable profile — image moderation belongs with Phase 12 hardening); Unsplash use-reporting happens when an image is placed in a slideshow (Phase 7), not at search.

**Phase 6 review list:**
- Fingerprint is sha256 of the bytes, not a perceptual hash: re-encoded or resized copies are not deduplicated, and the stock-photo filter only catches byte-identical copies of stock images Studio itself has downloaded (no external stock-hash database exists in the specs). A dHash would need an image decoder (sharp).
- Unsplash: its API terms forbid storing copies, so Unsplash results are stored as hotlinks (no S3 object) with attribution and the download_location tracking URL in licenseNotes. Reporting use to Unsplash must happen when an image is placed in a video (Phase 7 auto-populate), not at search time.
- Storyblocks download signing uses the download resource path including the item id (docs show only the search example); verify with a live key.
- A6.6 refresh cadence (30-day rescans, weekly stock delta, ETag/Last-Modified skip) needs a scheduler; manual refresh is built, the recurring schedule is not (Phase 11 cron alongside analytics polling). website_scans has no etag column, so the skip-if-unchanged rule would need a migration.
- A6.7 Enterprise DNS-TXT ownership verification and the 24h purge on disputed ownership are not built.
- Search embeds image descriptions, not pixels: images with no alt text/tags are embedded from their tags only (scraped images get the business's themes).
- Image generation is synchronous in the request (maxDuration 120s).
- businessId remains unverifiable against Core; all Feature D data is keyed per organisation, so an unverified id can only affect the caller's own data.

**Phase 4 security review — fixed:** Idempotency-Key now reserve-then-execute (SET NX before the handler; concurrent duplicates get 409, never a second execution; key released on failure) and bound to a request-body hash (422 on reuse with a different body); archive write scoped by organisationId; approve/reject moved to a new `studio:project:approve` capability so editors can't self-approve (Core must grant it to reviewers).

**Phase 4 review list:**
- businessId is accepted as given: Core's context contract (assumed) carries no business list, so Studio cannot verify the business belongs to the organisation (spec 7.14 expects Core-side validation).
- Plan tier comes from Core context `organisation.planTier`; unknown/missing → BASIC routing.
- REQUIRE_APPROVAL_FROM_ROLE and multi-step approval workflows are stored but not enforced yet (single-step approve/reject recorded as ApprovalTask).
- Preview/download URLs are S3 presigned URLs; CloudFront signed URLs (CDN_URL) not wired yet.
- No request rate limiting yet (planned with hardening, Phase 12).
- Script PATCH / script regenerate / render rerender (spec 8.3–8.4) not built: shot-level edit/regenerate covers the backlog items.

**Phase 3 review list:**
- Hive async moderation needs a public callback URL (Phase 4 API) — videos >90s currently FAIL the content-safety check (fail-closed) until then. Long-form (YouTube 4–8 min) cannot reach READY_FOR_REVIEW yet.
- Voice runs inside the per-shot asset job (not a separate parallel job as in spec 4.5 step 5); shots still run in parallel with each other.
- Treatments offered to Layer 2 = configured providers + TEXT_CARD. STOCK_FOOTAGE, AI_AVATAR, MOTION_GRAPHICS, USER_UPLOAD are unavailable until their adapters/flows exist.
- Script-safety REVIEW stops the run (no human review queue yet; fail closed).
- Codec check requires H.264 in MP4 but not the Baseline profile the spec names; Shotstack output profile is recorded in the check detail. Enforcing Baseline would fail every render until a re-encode step exists.
- Loudness is not normalised by the pipeline; renders outside −18..−10 LUFS go to QUALITY_FAILED (force-approvable).
- Text cards/captions use Shotstack's `html` asset (documented, deprecated in favour of rich-text); Phase 8 overlay translator replaces it.
- Local dev DB without Docker: `npm run db:local` (PGlite + pgvector); DATABASE_URL needs `&pgbouncer=true`.

**Phase 2 security review (independent agent) — all findings fixed:**
- HIGH: cost reserved at submit (estimate → provider_usage + costActualPence), settled to actual at completion, released on failure/cancel. Spend by synchronous providers is visible to caps even if the job is never polled.
- HIGH: router requires `request`; providers without a cost estimator are skipped (`no_cost_estimate`) instead of estimating 0.
- HIGH: half-open trial slot released when the kill switch / DB aborts a submit or the error is client-side; abandoned trials expire after 15 min.
- MEDIUM: pollTracked/cancelTracked take the caller's organisationId; other orgs' jobs return 404.
- MEDIUM: temporary / presigned URLs are redacted before responses are stored in provider_jobs.
- MEDIUM: S3 key segments validated (no '/', '..', leading '.').
- LOW: explicit Anthropic (120s) and OpenAI (180s) SDK timeouts.
- Runway FAILED tasks now report billed credits so charged failures stay counted.

**GATE 2 review list:**
- SPEC DRIFT — DALL-E 3 was removed from OpenAI's API on 2026-05-12. Using `gpt-image-2` (OpenAI's named replacement; `OPENAI_IMAGE_MODEL` to change).
- SPEC DRIFT — Suno (spec music primary) has no public API (checked Sept 2026). Music uses ElevenLabs Music (`elevenlabs-music`, `ELEVENLABS_MUSIC_MODEL`); see Operator decisions.
- SPEC DRIFT — Runway "Gen-4 Turbo" is image-to-video only; "Gen-4 Alpha" no longer exists. Text-only clips use `gen4.5` (12 credits/s vs 5); frame-seeded clips use `gen4_turbo`. Runway's API also hosts `veo3.1` (the spec's "Veo 3" for PLUS/ENTERPRISE is only reachable this way or via Vertex AI; `veo3` is gone).
- DEVIATION — ProviderAdapter `output.url` is optional (text/embedding results have no file). Adapters may add optional `estimateCostPence()` / `typicalLatencySec` for the router.
- DECISION — BASIC-plan AI_CLIP shots over 5s use the cheap list (spec 6.4 defines only ≤5s); AI_AVATAR gets D-ID↔HeyGen fallbacks (spec 6.1 "always at least one fallback").
- PRICING TO CONFIRM — ElevenLabs `eleven_multilingual_v2` priced at $0.10/1k chars; FX rate `STUDIO_USD_TO_GBP_RATE` is operator config (placeholder 0.75).
- Anthropic model default changed from `claude-sonnet-4-5` (starter .env.example) to `claude-sonnet-5`, the current Sonnet.
- Only Runway is configured for AI_CLIP today, so after 5 simulated Runway failures the router correctly returns NO_PROVIDER_AVAILABLE for STANDARD shots. A real fallback needs a Luma or Kling adapter (not in the Phase 2 backlog).

**GATE 1 review list (decisions taken — please confirm or correct):**
- DERIVED `provider_usage`: one row per (organisationId, provider, day) with job/succeeded/failed counts and costPence.
- DERIVED `style_memories`: one row per (org, business, signalType) with 5 signal types from spec 10.3 (hence "1–5 per business"); `reason` field for the 10.4 transparency requirement; soft-delete.
- DERIVED `video_library_categories`: self-referencing tree (slug, name, parentId, depth, sortOrder). Items keep a single `categoryId` as A7.3 specifies, although A3.4 says auto-classification places videos in 1–3 categories. Multi-category needs a join table if wanted.
- DERIVED `video_library_tags`: tag dictionary (name, usageCount). Items keep `tags String[]` per A7.3.
- DEVIATION `image_library.embedding` is nullable (spec: required). A required Unsupported column disables Prisma create(), and images are stored before their embedding exists.
- Kill-switch storage: Engagement keeps workspace freezes on Core's `Organisation`; Studio can't modify Core, so all four levels are system_flags keys (`studio.killSwitch`, `studio.frozenWorkspace.<org>`, `studio.killedProject.<id>`, `studio.disabledProvider.<id>`).
- Core context contract CONFIRMED by the operator 2026-09-27 (was assumed): `GET /api/internal/context/:userId` → `{ organisation: {id, name?, planTier?}, memberships: [{organisationId, role}], capabilities: string[] }`. Validated with zod; any other shape returns 502. JWT user id read from `userId` claim, falling back to `sub`.
- Admin capability names (`studio:admin:kill-switch:read|write`, `:providers`, `:library`, `:moderation`) are Studio-side proposals under the backlog's `studio:admin:*`; Core must grant them.
- tenant.ts KNOWN RISK (security review): capability/membership changes within one org take up to 5 min to apply (spec 16.1 cache). Org switches are re-read from Core immediately. No explicit JWT `algorithms` allow-list yet: please confirm which alg Core signs with (e.g. RS256) and it will be pinned.
- Embeddings are `vector(1536)` per spec; text-embedding-3-large must be called with `dimensions: 1536` (its native size is 3072). Phase 2.5.

**Open questions:**
- RESOLVED 2026-09-27 (migrations and queries work wherever pgvector lives; see [OPS-pgvector]) — pgvector on the shared cluster: on a cluster without pgvector, migrations install it into `studio` (verified). If PostMind Core has ALREADY installed `vector` in `public`, `CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS` is a no-op and the `vector(1536)` columns will not resolve under `search_path=studio`, so the init migration fails loudly. Ops needs to confirm the production cluster's state before first deploy.
- Node version: CLAUDE.md says Node 20 LTS. CI runs 20. This dev machine runs Node 24, which works, but the spec baseline is 20.
